whether the N-1 metabolite identified in the HPLC chromatograms was the AS(N-1)3’ or 
AS(N-1)5’ metabolite. However, based on in vitro metabolic stability studies and metabolite 
profiling of in vitro human serum and liver S9 fractions, the N-1 metabolite in human plasma 
was expected to be the AS(N-1)3’ metabolite. 
14.1.2.3. Study 319N-1520: CYP450 Isoforms Involved in 
the Metabolism of Fitusiran 
Study 319N-1520 evaluated the potential human CYP450 isoforms involved in the metabolism 
of ALN-AT3SC using cDNA-expressed enzymes. The objective of this study was to identify if 
any of the cytochrome P450 enzymes (CYP) are involved in the metabolism of fitusiran by using 
a panel of recombinant cytochrome P450 enzymes (rCYPs). 
In this study, fitusiran was incubated in triplicate with individual rCYPs: rCYP1A2, rCYP2B6, 
rCYP2C8, rCYP2C9, rCYP2C19, rCYP2D6, rCYP3A4 and rCYP3A5. The reaction mixture 
consisted of a specific rCYP, 100mM potassium phosphate buffer (pH 7.4), 1mM NADPH, and 
fitusiran at 2 concentrations, 800 ng/mL and 8,000 ng/mL. Reactions with fitusiran were 
terminated at 0 and 45 minutes with ice-cold methanol (1:1 v/v) and with the positive controls at 
30 minutes with ice-cold acetonitrile containing internal standard (1:1 v/v). Probe substrates for 
each of the rCYP enzymes were included in the study as positive controls. The negative control 
incubations consisted of fitusiran with rCYPs but without NADPH. Fitusiran was analyzed by 
liquid chromatography-time of flight-mass spectrometry (LC-TOF-MS). Positive control 
substrates were analyzed by ultra-high-performance liquid chromatography-mass 
spectrometry/mass spectrometry (UHPLC-MS/MS). Peak area ratios were used to calculate the 
percent parent drug remaining at 45 or 30 minutes. 
The results showed that the percentage of fitusiran remaining after 45 minutes ranged from 
~90% to ~100% for all rCYPs tested in the presence of NADPH. In contrast, positive control 
incubations showed significant metabolism as parent compound was depleted for rCYP2C9, 
rCYP2D6, and rCYP3A4/5, with 35.9% to 68.0% remaining for the other rCYPs tested. 
These results indicated that fitusiran is not a substrate of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, 
CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP3A4, or CYP3A5. 
14.1.2.4. Study AT3-NCD13-014 (CYP Inhibition) 
Study AT3-NCD13-014 evaluated the inhibitory potential of fitusiran against eight principal 
human CYP isozymes using human liver microsomes: CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, 
CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4/5. 
In this study, CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP2D6 
activity was investigated using phenacetin, coumarin, bupropion, amodiaquine, tolbutamide, 
S-mephenytoin, and dextromethorphan as probe substrates, respectively. CYP3A4/5 activity was 
investigated using both midazolam and testosterone as probe substrates. The inhibitory activity 
was evaluated using pooled human liver microsomes and was measured at 10 fitusiran 
concentrations (0 to 10,000 μg/mL), in triplicate. Reaction was initiated by addition of NADPH 
and following incubation at 37℃ (3 to 30 minutes for different probe substrates). The reaction 
was terminated by addition of acetonitrile. The metabolites of the probe substrates in pooled 
Reference ID: 5560526

===== PAGE 188 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
167 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
samples (acetaminophen for CYP1A2, 7-hydroxycoumarin for CYP2A6, hydroxybupropion for 
CYP2B6, N-desethylamodiaquine for CYP2C8, 4-hydroxytolbutamide for CYP2C9, 
4’-hydroxymephenytoin for CYP2C19, dextrorphan for CYP2D6, 6β-hydroxytestosterone and 
1’-hydroxymidazolam for CYP3A4/5) were measured using electrospray ionization techniques. 
Results 
There were no changes in activity as compared to the vehicle control for CYP1A2, CYP2A6, 
CYP2B6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4/5 (both midazolam and testosterone as 
substrates), indicating that fitusiran was not an inhibitor of any of these CYPs. Fitusiran showed 
inhibition of CYP2C8, with a calculated half maximal inhibitory concentration (IC50) value for 
fitusiran of ~800 μg/mL, indicating that fitusiran was not an inhibitor of CYP2C8 enzymes at 
clinically relevant concentrations (mean maximum plasma concentration at the highest 
recommended clinical dose of 50 mg is 84.1 ng/mL [LTE15174], which is ~9500-fold lower than 
the CYP2C8 IC50 value). The calculated IC50 values for positive controls were consistent with 
reported literature values. 
To summarize, fitusiran is unlikely to inhibit any of these tested CYPs at clinically relevant 
concentrations. 
14.1.2.5. Study 319N-1517 (CYP Induction) 
Study 319N-1517 evaluated the potential of fitusiran to induce CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4 
in sandwich cultured human hepatocytes. 
Evaluation of the degree of CYP induction was performed by the following two methods: (i) 
measurement of mRNA levels by reverse transcription-polymerase chain reaction (TaqMan), and 
(ii) measurement of enzyme activities using isoform-specific probe substrates. Cultured human 
hepatocytes from three separate adult donors were cultured on collagen-coated plates and 
incubated with vehicle alone, positive and negative control compounds, or fitusiran for 48 or 72 
hours (mRNA and enzyme activities, respectively). Fitusiran was evaluated at 4 concentrations: 
0.005, 0.25, 2.5, and 25 μg/mL. Activity levels of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 were 
measured in situ with a cocktail of phenacetin, bupropion, and midazolam using qualified liquid 
chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry (LC MS/MS) methods (details in 319N-1517). The 
criteria for positive CYP induction were increases in mRNA levels 2-fold above the vehicle 
control or enzyme activity ≥40% (or 0.4-fold) of the relevant positive control induction. 
Results 
Hepatocytes treated with fitusiran showed no statistically significant increase in CYP1A2, 
CYP2B6, or CYP3A4 mRNA levels at the concentrations tested, and no increased CYP1A2, 
CYP2B6, or CYP3A4 enzyme activity under the conditions utilized in this study. Based on these 
results, fitusiran appeared unlikely to be a CYP inducer at concentrations that were at or below 
25 μg/mL (mean maximum plasma concentration at the highest recommended clinical dose of 
50 mg is 84.1 ng/mL [LTE15174], which is ~300-fold lower than the highest concentration of 
25 μg/mL tested in this study). 
To summarize, fitusiran is unlikely to induce any of these tested CYPs at clinically relevant 
concentrations.

NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
168 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
14.1.2.6. Study Alnylam-03-22Jul2016: Transporter 
Inhibition and Substrate Evaluations 
Study Alnylam-03-22Jul2016 evaluated whether fitusiran is a substrate and inhibitor of efflux 
and uptake transporters, through in vitro interaction studies of fitusiran with human BSEP, 
BCRP and MDR1 efflux (ABC) transporters, and with human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, 
OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 uptake transporters. The objective of this study 
was to evaluate potential interaction of fitusiran with 11 human efflux and uptake transporters. 
The transporters evaluated, assays performed, and fitusiran concentrations tested are summarized 
in Table 109. 
Table 109. Transporters Studied and Transporter Assays 
 
Source: Table 5, Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Studies 
In this study, vesicular transport inhibition and substrate assays were performed with inside-out 
membrane vesicles prepared from cells overexpressing human ATP-binding cassette (ABC) 
transporters in human embryonic kidney cell line 293 (HEK293). Uptake transporter inhibition 
and substrate assays were performed using Madin Darby canine kidney cells II (MDCKII) or 
HEK293 cells stably expressing the respective uptake transporters. 
Results 
Vesicular Transport Inhibition Assays 
Fitusiran did not influence the BCRP-, BSEP-, and multidrug resistance protein 1 (MDR-1)-
mediated probe substrate accumulation at the concentrations evaluated. 
Reference ID: 5560526
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  

===== PAGE 190 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
169 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Vesicular Transport Substrate Assays 
ATP-dependent accumulation of fitusiran was similar in the presence of ATP and AMP, 
indicating no active accumulation of fitusiran in transporter (BCRP, BSEP and MDR1) 
expressing or control vesicles. 
Uptake Transporter Inhibition Assays 
Fitusiran inhibited the organic cation transporter (OCT)2-mediated metformin transport by 24% 
at 487 μg/mL. Fitusiran did not influence the multidrug and toxin extrusion transporter 
(MATE)1-, MATE2-K-, organic anion-transporting polypeptide (OATP)1B1-, OATP1B3-, 
organic anion transporter (OAT)1-, OAT3- and OCT1-mediated probe substrate accumulation at 
the applied concentrations. 
Uptake Transporter Substrate Assays 
Accumulation of fitusiran was similar in the OAT1-, OAT3-and OCT2-expressing and the 
control cells, indicating no active accumulation of fitusiran. Transporter-specific accumulation of 
fitusiran was observed in cells expressing OATP1B1 (2.45-fold at 487 μg/mL), OATP1B3 (2.51-
fold at 487 μg/mL), MATE1 (2.24-fold at 48.7 μg/mL) and MATE2-K (8.64-fold at 48.7 μg/mL, 
2.39-fold at 4.87 μg/mL, and 0.81-fold at 0.487 μg/mL). These results indicated that fitusiran 
was likely a substrate of MATE2-K uptake transporters at high concentrations, but probably not 
at clinically relevant concentrations (no accumulation observed at 0.487 μg/mL [487 ng/mL], 
which is 5.79-fold higher than the mean maximum plasma concentration of 84.1 ng/mL observed 
at the highest recommended clinical dose of 50 mg [LTE15174]). 
To summarize, fitusiran is unlikely to be a substrate or inhibitor of the tested efflux and uptake 
transporters at clinically relevant concentrations. 
14.2. In Vivo Studies 
14.2.1. Single Ascending Dose and Multiple Ascending 
Dose PK Study in Healthy Subjects 
14.2.1.1. Phase 1 Study TDR14767 
Study TDR14767 was a phase 1 single ascending dose and multiple ascending dose safety, 
tolerability, and pharmacokinetics study of subcutaneously administered fitusiran in healthy 
adult volunteers and hemophilia A or B subjects (moderate or severe hemophilia). The primary 
objective was to evaluate the safety and tolerability of fitusiran when administered to healthy 
adult male subjects and when administered to male subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia 
A or B. 
Reference ID: 5560526

===== PAGE 191 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
170 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Study Design 
This study consisted of 4 parts: Part A, a single-ascending-dose phase in healthy subjects; Part B, 
a multiple-ascending-dose phase in subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia A or B; Part C, 
an exploratory multiple-dose phase in male subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia A or B; 
and Part D, a multiple-dose phase in subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia A or B with 
inhibitors. 
The doses were: Part A: 0.03 mg/kg (single dose); Part B: 0.015, 0.045, or 0.075 mg/kg once 
weekly (QW) (total of 3 doses); Part C: 0.225, 0.45, 0.9, or 1.8 mg/kg, or 80 mg QM (total of 3 
doses); Part D: 50 or 80 mg QM (total of 3 doses). A total of 46 subjects were enrolled (45 
treated with fitusiran, 1 placebo). 
Intensive PK sampling was performed in Part A (healthy subjects) following a single dose of 
fitusiran, in Part B (subjects with hemophilia A or B) after the first (Day 0) and third QW (Day 
14) dose of fitusiran, and in Parts C and D (subjects with hemophilia A or B) after the first (Day 
0) and third QM (Day 56) dose of fitusiran. PK sampling timepoints were: predose, then 0.083, 
0.167, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 48, 72, and 168 hours postdose for 0.015- to 0.075-mg/kg dose 
cohorts; and predose, then 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, and 24 hours postdose for 0.225-mg/kg to 
1.8-mg/kg and 50- and 80-mg dose cohorts. Urine samples for PK analysis were collected from 0 
to 6, 6 to 12, and 12 to 24 hours after the first and last doses. 
Samples for AT and thrombin generation (TG) measurements were collected at the following 
timepoints: Part A (single dose): at screening, on Day (D)0 (predose/baseline), then D1, D2, D3, 
D7, D10, D14, D21, D28, D42, and D56; Part B (3×QW doses): at screening, on D0 
(predose/baseline), then D1, D2, D3, D7 (predose), D8, D9, D14 (predose), and D15; Parts C and 
D (3×QM doses): at screening, on D0 (predose/baseline), then D1, D7, D14, D28 (predose), 
D29, D42, D56 (predose), D57, D70, D84, D98, and D112. All samples for ADA assessment 
were collected at predose/trough time points on D0 (Part A), D0, D7, and D14 (Part B), and D0, 
D28, and D57 (Parts C and D). 
Results 
Plasma Pharmacokinetics 
Fitusiran plasma PK parameters following single SC administration of fitusiran to healthy 
volunteers at 0.03 mg/kg (Part A), to subjects with hemophilia after single and multiple (n=3) 
doses at 0.015 to 0.045 mg/kg QW (Part B), at 0.225 to 1.8 mg/kg and 80 mg QM (Part C), and 
50 and 80 mg QM (Part D), are presented in Table 110 (single dose data) and Table 111 
(multiple dose data).

Overall, the PK data indicate that following SC administration to subjects with hemophilia, 
fitusiran exposures (Cmax, AUC) increased with dose on both PK assessment days 0 and 56 (after 
first and third dose); plasma half-lives were similar on Day 0 (~3 to 5 hours) and Day 56 (~2 to 
6 hours). Because plasma half-life was very short relative to the dosing interval (QW or QM), no 
significant accumulation was expected, and the data showed no fitusiran accumulation with 
monthly dosing.

proportionality on Day 0 and Day 56. Representative examples of fitusiran concentration-time

14.2.2. PK/PD Studies in Patients (Five Studies) 
14.2.2.1. Phase 1/2 Study LTE14762 
Study LTE14762 was a phase 1/2 long term extension study (up to 7 years) in adult subjects with 
hemophilia who had completed the phase 1 study TDR14767. The primary objective was to 
evaluate the long-term safety and tolerability of fitusiran in male subjects with moderate or 
severe hemophilia A or B. 
Study Design 
This study was an open-label extension study. As mentioned above, Study TDR14767 was 
conducted in 4 parts. Subjects from Part B rolled over into the LTE1462 study after completing 
Day 70 in the parent study. Subjects from Parts C and D were expected to roll over into the 
LTE14762 study approximately 1 month after completing the 3 monthly doses of study drug in 
the parent study, thereby maintaining their monthly dosing schedule. 
Reference ID: 5560526
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
   
   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

===== PAGE 201 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
180 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Therefore, the fitusiran doses evaluated in this study were 50 mg and 80 mg SC, which were the 
doses evaluated in the parent study TDR14767. Doses in this study were initially administered in 
terms of fitusiran sodium salt, but later as fitusiran free acid. Dosing frequency during the period 
that PK samples were collected (up to 2 years from enrollment) was QM. A dosing pause period 
for the original dose and regimen was implemented on October 30, 2020, due to thrombotic 
events observed in other clinical studies within the fitusiran clinical program. Later, a 50 mg 
Q2M regimen was introduced as part of a risk mitigation strategy against potential thrombotic 
events since November 25, 2020. 
Intensive plasma PK sampling was performed on Day 1, Day 361 (Month 12), and Day 721 
(Month 24). The sampling timepoints were: predose, then 0.5, 2, 4, 8, and 24 hours postdose. 
Urine samples (0 to 6, 6 to 12, and 12 to 24 hours) were collected on Day 721. 
Samples for AT activity and TG measurements were collected at the following timepoints: (i) 
subjects who remained on original dose regimen: D1, then monthly up to Month (M) 24, then 
M30, M36, M42 and M48; (ii) subjects who changed to modified dose regimen: D1 (day of dose 
restart), then monthly from post-restart (PR) M1 to PRM12, then PRM16, PRM20, PRM24, 
PRM28, PR M32, and PRM36. All AT and TG samples were collected predose. 
Samples for ADA evaluation were collected on D1, M1, M2, M3, M6, M9, M12, M15, M18, 
M21, M24, M36, M48, M60, M72, and M84 for the original dose regimens, and PRD1, PRM6, 
PRM12, PRM24, and PRM36 for the post-restart dose regimens. All ADA samples were 
collected predose.

Pharmacodynamics 
Monthly dosing with fitusiran (original dose and regimen, PD Set 1) resulted in a change in AT 
activity to 14.4% of baseline starting at Month 1 and persisting over several months. In the 
revised AT-DR regimen (PD Set 2), the change in AT activity was 23.3% of baseline. There 
were 3 subjects who discontinued the study because their AT levels were <15%. The rest of the 
AT activity levels were maintained between 15 to 35%. 
Consistent with fitusiran’s mechanism of action, TG increased with AT lowering, reaching a 
peak mean of 74.42nM by endpoint evaluation that remained elevated during the course of 
fitusiran treatment for the overall subjects in PD Set 1. In PD Set 2, TG reached a mean of 
88.08nM. 
Immunogenicity 
Two subjects developed treatment-induced ADA; one was positive at one timepoint (Month 10) 
with a titer of 200, and the other was positive at Month 48, Month 60, and Month 72, with a titer 
of 400 at Month 48 and 100 (equivalent to minimum required dilution [MRD] of the assay) at the 
other timepoints.

14.2.2.2. Phase 3 Pivotal Study EFC14768 
Study EFC14768 was a phase 3 study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of fitusiran in subjects 
with hemophilia A or B, with inhibitory antibodies to Factor VIII or IX. The primary objective 
was to evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with BPAs, as 
determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes. 
Study Design 
Sixty subjects were enrolled in this study, 41 on fitusiran and 19 on bypassing agents (BPA) on 
demand). PK and PD samples were only collected from the fitusiran-treated subjects. 
The fitusiran SC dose evaluated in this study was 80 mg QM, except in 3 subjects from mainland 
China who received a single first dose of 50 mg, before being switched to 80 mg QM. 
Fitusiran treatment duration was 9 months. 
Intensive PK sampling (predose, then 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, and 24 hours postdose) was performed 
after the first fitusiran dose in East Asian subjects, whilst sparse PK samples (4 hours postdose 
and trough) were collected from all subjects at visits specified in the study protocol. Urine 
samples for PK analysis were collected at 0 to 6, 6 to 12, and 12 to 24 hours after the first 
fitusiran dose. 
Samples for AT activity and TG measurements were collected at screening, then D1 (baseline), 
D2 (East Asian only), D8 (East Asian only), D15, D29 (M1), D43, D57 (M2), then monthly up 
to M9. All AT and TG samples, except D2, D8, and D15, were collected predose. Sampling 
timepoints for ADA analysis in phase 3 studies are summarized in Section 14.4.1 
Results 
Pharmacokinetics 
A summary of single-dose PK parameters is presented in Table 114.

Pharmacodynamics 
Monthly dosing with fitusiran resulted in a sustained reduction of AT activity, reaching 15.9% of 
baseline by Day 29. Consistent with fitusiran’s mechanism of action, TG increased with AT 
reduction, reaching a peak mean of 46.7nM by Day 43 (540% change from baseline) that

sustained improvement of coagulation potential. 
Immunogenicity 
One subject developed treatment-induced ADA at one timepoint, with a titer of 50, equivalent to 
minimum required dilution (MRD) of the assay, indicating that fitusiran has low potential to 
elicit an immunogenic response.

14.2.2.4. Phase 3 Pivotal Study EFC15110  
Study EFC15110 was an open-label, multinational switching study to describe the efficacy and 
safety of fitusiran prophylaxis in subjects with hemophilia A and B previously receiving factor or 
bypassing agent prophylaxis. The primary objective was to characterize the frequency of 
bleeding episodes while receiving fitusiran treatment, relative to the frequency of bleeding 
episodes while receiving factor concentrate or bypassing agent (BPA) prophylaxis. 
Study Design 
PK and PD samples were only collected during the fitusiran treatment phase of the study.

Fitusiran doses evaluated in this study were 80 mg QM and 50 mg Q2M (administered in terms 
of free acid), and fitusiran treatment duration was 7 months. Intensive PK sampling was 
performed after the first fitusiran dose in East Asian subjects. Sparse PK samples (4 hours 
postdose and trough) were collected from all subjects. Since the change in dose regimen from 
80 mg QM to 50 mg Q2M, introduced as part of a risk mitigation strategy against potential 
thrombotic events, was only made towards the end of the study when the majority of subjects 
had already completed their 7-month fitusiran treatment period, all the PK data from this study 
are with the 80 mg QM dose. 
Sample collection timepoints for intensive and sparse plasma PK and urine PK analyses from 
East Asian subjects after the first fitusiran dose were the same as in Study EFC14768. Samples 
for AT activity and TG measurements were collected at screening, then D1 (baseline), D2 (East 
Asian only), D8 (East Asian only), D15, D29 (M1), D43, D57 (M2), then monthly up to M7. All 
AT and TG samples, except D2, D8, and D15, were collected predose. Sampling timepoints for 
ADA analysis in phase 3 studies are summarized in Section 14.4.1.

Pharmacodynamics 
Monthly dosing with fitusiran resulted in a sustained reduction of AT levels, reaching 16.6% of 
baseline by Day 29 that persisted over several months. The lowest AT level reached was 9.4% of 
baseline at Day 169, with 12.7% for Cohort A (inhibitor) and 7.8% for Cohort B (noninhibitor), 
respectively. 
Consistent with fitusiran’s mechanism of action, TG increased with AT reduction, reaching a 
peak mean of 62.6nM (472% change from baseline) by endpoint evaluation (defined as the last 
nonmissing postbaseline assessment on or before EOS/ET visit for fitusiran 80 mg prophylaxis 
period) that remained elevated during the course of fitusiran treatment for the overall subjects. 
The peak TG levels were 54.1nM (564% change from baseline) for subjects in Cohort A 
(inhibitor) and 66.5nM (429% change from baseline) for the subjects in Cohort B (noninhibitor), 
respectively. 
Immunogenicity 
Two subjects had treatment-induced ADA at one timepoint each. The ADA titer in each case was 
50 (equivalent to the MRD of the assay).  
14.2.2.5. Phase 3 Pivotal Study LTE15174 
Study LTE15174 was an open-label long-term safety and efficacy study of fitusiran in subjects 
with hemophilia A or B, with or without inhibitory antibodies to factor VIII or IX. This study 
used the AT-DR. The primary objective was to characterize the long-term safety and tolerability 
of fitusiran. The primary endpoints of this study were the incidence, severity, relatedness, and 
seriousness of adverse events (AEs), and laboratory assessments. 
Study Design 
This study enabled subjects who had completed any of the phase 3 studies of fitusiran to 
continue to be evaluated for long-term safety and efficacy. It was initially planned as a long-term 
extension study for subjects from phase 3 studies EFC14768, EFC14769, and EFC15110 at the 
same dose regimen (80 mg QM) as the parent studies. The majority of subjects from the parent 
studies had already rolled over into LTE15174 when dosing was temporarily paused to evaluate 
a revised AT-DR. Unlike the dose regimen in the parent studies, which targeted AT reduction of 
≥85% in the majority of subjects, the postpause strategy targeted AT activity values between 
15% to 35% with fitusiran treatment. 
The final dose regimens evaluated in LTE15174 were: 20 mg Q2M, 20 mg QM, 50 mg Q2M, 
50 mg QM, and 80 mg QM. All doses were administered in terms of fitusiran free acid. 
Following the dosing pause, all subjects except 11, who had AT activity of 15 to 35% at 80 mg 
QM regimen, were restarted on fitusiran treatment at 50 mg Q2M. Of these 11 subjects, 4 later 
required a dose reduction, but the other 7 remained on the 80 mg QM dose regimen. Subjects 
who had AT values >35% after 2 to 3 doses at 50 mg Q2M, were dose escalated to 50 mg QM 
(and if needed, to 80 mg QM); if they had AT values <15%, they were dose de-escalated to 
20 mg Q2M (and if needed, escalated to 20 mg QM from 20 mg Q2M), as appropriate, based on 
the criteria shown in Figure 5 in section 6.1.3. Subjects who had AT activity <15% at 20 mg 
Q2M were discontinued from the study. 
Reference ID: 5560526

===== PAGE 212 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
191 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Fitusiran dosing in all previous clinical studies was conducted using a glass vial and syringe 
device, and the same device was used to deliver the different doses (20 mg, 50 mg, 80 mg) in this 
study. In addition, a 50 mg fitusiran prefilled pen (PFP) device was also evaluated in this study. 
Intensive PK sampling was performed in the following 3 subgroups of subjects: (a) after the first 
50-mg dose in East Asians (at least 12 each from mainland China [mainly de novo subjects] and 
South Korea, and all subjects [who consented to intensive PK sampling] from Japan and 
Taiwan), and Non-East Asians (at least 12 subjects from rest of the world [ROW]); (b) after the 
first 20-mg dose to at least 6 East Asian subjects (from any East Asian country or region 
[mainland China, Japan, South Korea, Taiwan]), and at least 6 Non-East Asian subjects (from 
ROW), and (c) 12 subjects (any region of the world) for PK comparison between 50-mg dose 
administered first using a glass vial and syringe device (all 12 subjects), and at the next dose 
(Q2M or QM), using a PFP device (all 12 subjects). Eleven (11) subjects completed intensive 
sampling following administration by both devices. Intensive PK samples were not collected 
from any of the subjects who remained on the 80 mg QM dose in this study. Sparse PK samples 
(at 4 hours postdose and trough) were collected from all subjects. Urine PK samples were 
collected from 7 subjects in the 50 mg Q2M group only. 
Sample collection timepoints for intensive and sparse plasma PK and urine PK analyses were the 
same as for the other phase 3 studies. Details of the AT and TG sampling times for subjects 
under different dose regimens are provided in the schedule of assessments of LTE15174 
protocol, but in general they were: (a) D1 (baseline), D15, D29 (M1), M2, M3, then every 3 
months up to M24, then every 6 months up to M48, for subjects who remained on original dose 
regimen; and (b) PRD1 (day of dose restart), then monthly from PRM1 to PRM12, then every 4 
months up to PRM36, then PRM42 and PRM48, for subjects who changed to AT-DR. All AT 
and TG samples, except Day 15, were collected predose. Sampling timepoints for ADA analysis 
in phase 3 studies are summarized in Section 14.4.1.

Pharmacodynamics 
The fitusiran revised AT-DR was successful at maintaining AT levels within the targeted AT 
range. Most subjects were able to achieve AT levels between 15 to 35% with the revised AT-DR. 
Overall, 200 of 213 rolled-over subjects (93%) required no or 1 dose change to maintain AT 
activity between 15 to 35%; 167 (78%) subjects were on Q2M regimens. None of the subjects 
who initiated dosing at 50 mg Q2M needed a dose higher than 50 mg QM to achieve AT activity 
in the target 15 to 35% range. Of subjects at the lowest dose evaluated (20 mg Q2M), 11.7% 
repeatedly had AT <15% and thus required fitusiran discontinuation. 
Under the revised AT-DR, dosing with fitusiran resulted in a sustained lowering of AT activity 
levels. The mean (SD) AT level was 23.5% (4.64%) during the Primary Efficacy Period. The 
results showed that the majority of subjects were within the target AT range of 15 to 35% at 
steady-state and remained within the range for the duration of the study under the revised AT-
DR, regardless of the actual fitusiran dose. Following discontinuation of fitusiran treatment, 
fitusiran effects (reduced AT activity) persisted over several months. In the majority of subjects 
AT had recovered to >60% in 6 months. 
Immunogenicity 
Overall, 4 out of 5 confirmed ADA-positive subjects had treatment-induced ADA, which were 
generally transient. One subject was ADA-positive only at study entry and not at subsequent 
timepoints. One subject had persistent treatment-induced ADA with a peak ADA titer of 100 and 
last titer of 50.

Given the complexity of hemophilia, no evident impact on annualized bleeding rate (ABR) was

studies. The observed treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were consistent with those 
expected of the underlying disease and the known safety profile of fitusiran. 
Summary of Immunogenicity Assessment 
The clinical findings support that fitusiran has a low immunogenicity risk relative to ADA 
impact on clinical outcomes. Subjects who developed ADA had a low titer response that did not 
meaningfully influence PK, PD (AT activity), efficacy as assessed by ABR, or safety parameters 
when compared to subjects who did not develop ADA. 
14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment 
14.5.1. Applicant’s Population Kinetic-
Pharmacodynamic Model Analysis 
Upon single or multiple dose administration of fitusiran in nonhuman primates (NHP) and in 
man, plasma concentrations decline rapidly and are below the lower limit of quantitation in 1 to 
2 days postdose, while suppression of the target protein, antithrombin (AT), is sustained for days, 
Reference ID: 5560526
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
(b) (6)
(b) (6)
(b) (6)
(b) (6)

===== PAGE 224 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
203 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
highlighting the disconnect between plasma concentrations and PD effect. This lack of 
correlation is to be expected based on the mechanism of action of fitusiran, i.e., (i) the GalNAc-
mediated targeted delivery of the double-stranded small interfering ribonucleic acid (siRNA)-
conjugate to the liver following subcutaneous administration; (ii) uptake of the siRNA into 
hepatocytes; and (iii) binding of the antisense strand of the siRNA to the RNA-induced silencing 
complex (RISC), causing specific degradation of the AT mRNA in the hepatocytes, the primary 
source of AT production. The time taken for this process to result in measurable decreases in 
plasma AT levels, including the time for the elimination of preexisting AT, explains the duration 
of onset of pharmacological effect of fitusiran following administration. Further, since a single 
RISC-bound antisense strand in the hepatocyte can cause the catalytic degradation of multiple 
target AT mRNAs, the duration of the pharmacology of fitusiran is significantly longer than 
what might be estimated based on plasma PK. Based on the biology and mechanism of action, it 
is clear that liver concentrations of fitusiran are linked to the gradual decrease in serum AT and 
the ensuing long duration of AT suppression. 
Since liver concentrations are not practical to measure in man and were not measured in 
monkeys at pharmacologic doses of fitusiran, the Applicant used allometric scaling to predict 
liver concentrations of fitusiran in both NHPs and humans. This is based on other siRNA 
molecules (where liver biopsies in NHP were collected) with similar physicochemical 
characteristics to fitusiran, for which observed dose-proportional liver concentrations scale 
allometrically in rats and monkeys. It was noted that the liver PK of siRNA molecules scale 
allometrically between species with a clearance exponent of 0.75 and a volume exponent of 1.0. 
The overall modeling strategy was executed in a stepwise manner as follows: (1) fitusiran liver 
PK parameters, which were estimated based on fitusiran liver concentrations in rats, were 
allometrically scaled to humans; (2) fitusiran concentrations in the RISC complex (fitusiran-
RISC) were estimated based on scaled liver PK parameters and observed PD; (3) fitusiran-RISC 
concentrations were linked to AT lowering, with an inhibitory effect on synthesis rate of AT 
activity. A schematic of the modeling strategy is shown in Figure 35. 
Figure 35. Schematic Representation of PopK-PD Modeling Strategy 
 
Source: study-poh1133, Figure 6 
Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; Kdeg, degradation rate of AT; Ksyn, synthesis rate of AT; PK, pharmacokinetics; RISC, RNA-
induced silencing complex 
The fitusiran population kinetic-pharmacodynamic (PopK-PD) model (POH1133) was first 
developed based on plasma AT activity data from 41 hemophilia A or B subjects enrolled in 
Study ALN-AT3SC-001 (Parts B, C and D) and ALN-AT3-002. AT activity was measured

biweekly for Study ALN-AT3SC-001, and monthly for Study ALN-AT3SC-002. Fitusiran liver 
concentrations in rats were allometrically scaled to predict human liver PK. Fitusiran 
concentrations in the RISC complex (fitusiran-RISC) were predicted based on total liver PK and 
observed PD, where fitusiran-RISC concentrations had an inhibitory effect on synthesis rate of 
AT activity. Simulations of steady-state residual AT activity (at 1 month after the fifth dose) in 
1000 virtual hemophilia patients receiving monthly doses of fitusiran over a dose range of 1 to 
140 mg were conducted to guide the dose selection for pivotal trials. As shown in Figure 36, a 
sharp decline in mean steady-state AT lowering is predicted as fitusiran dose increases from 
1 mg to 20 mg. A further increase in dose from 20 mg to 50 mg shows a less rapid decline in AT 
activity. At doses greater than 80 mg, the dose-response curve reaches an asymptote, suggesting 
that any increase in dose beyond 80 mg will cause minimal further decline in AT activity. In 
addition, less variability in AT lowering is predicted at 80 mg (95th to 5th percentile of 77% to 
92%) compared to 50 mg (95th to 5th percentile of 72% to 90%) (Table 130). The 80 mg QM 
dosing regimen was selected for later stage clinical trials based on these simulation results as 
well as the observed safety, efficacy, and pharmacodynamic profile in trial TDR14767.

In October 2020, when the phase 1/2 (LTE14762), phase 3 (EFC14768, EFC14769, EFC15110), 
and phase 3 extension (LTE15174) studies were ongoing, concerns regarding thrombotic events 
potentially related to fitusiran resulted in placing the ongoing clinical studies on a voluntary 
pause by the Sponsor to evaluate risk factors. All available clinical data were analyzed to assess 
the potential role of AT reduction and the associated risk of thrombotic events, as well as bleeds. 
An optimized dose regimen targeting AT activity levels between 15% and 35% was 
implemented. The rationale for the selection of these targets was based on review of thrombotic 
event incidence as well as bleeding data at different AT activity levels. The clinical data, along 
with PopK-PD modeling, was used to assess doses and dose regimens that would achieve and 
maintain AT activity between 15% and 35%. The PopK-PD model (POH0721) was updated 
based on data from 45 healthy volunteers and hemophilia A or B subjects enrolled in Study 
ALN-AT3SC-001 and ALN-AT3SC-002. This dataset included 3 healthy volunteers, 34 subjects 
with hemophilia A, and 8 subjects with hemophilia B. Amongst the hemophilia subjects, 25 
subjects were without inhibitors and 17 subjects were with inhibitors. The updated model 
structure was identical to the first PopK-PD model. AT activity data until week 24 from phase 3 
trials (EFC14768, EFC14769, and EFC15110) were applied to validate the model. Simulation 
analysis was performed to guide the dose regimen selection which could achieve target AT 
activity range of 15% to 35%. 
The simulation results showed that with a starting dose of 80 mg QM, the minimum AT activity 
in virtual subjects could go down to 6.9%, where for the 50 mg Q2M regimen, the minimum AT 
activity would remain above 10% (Table 131 and Figure 37). Therefore, based on the simulation 
results, the starting dose was revised to 50 mg Q2M (instead of 80 mg QM), with an option to 
sequentially escalate to 50 mg QM and 80 mg QM if AT activity was >35%. This revised dose 
regimen was implemented in December 2020 across studies (except EFC14768 and EFC14769, 
which were nearly complete at the time of the voluntary dosing pause).

The model structure is shown in Figure 38. Based on the mechanism of action of fitusiran, the 
liver pharmacokinetic parameters (i.e., absorption rate [Ka], clearance from liver central 
compartment [CL], liver intercompartmental clearance [CL2], liver central volume [V2] and liver 
peripheral volume [V3]) were predicted by allometric scaling from rat liver PK and were fixed in 
the current PopK-PD model. The minimum uptake into RISC compartment (minQ) was fixed to 
0.0225 g/h and the volume of RISC compartment (RV), that is V2+V3, was fixed to be 9375 g. 
The bioavailability was set to 1 and the turnover rate of fitusiran from peripheral liver 
compartment into RISC compartment Q was assumed to be dose-dependent and defined as 
follows: 
Equation 1. Turnover Rate of Fitusiran from Peripheral Liver Compartment into RISC 
Compartment Q 
 
Qmax is the maximum turnover rate of fitusiran from the peripheral liver compartment and θQ is 
the hill coefficient correlating the impact of dose on the turnover rate of fitusiran from the 
peripheral compartment. Qmax and θQ were estimated. POP is a conditional variable where 
POP=1 if the subject is de-escalated to the 20 mg Q2M dose and POP=0 otherwise. The 
dynamics of AT activity were described by an indirect response model linking the predicted 
human liver concentrations to suppression of synthesis rate of AT through an effect compartment 
representing fitusiran loaded onto RISC. The key parameters for AT dynamics such as CLR 
(clearance from RISC compartment), Kout (AT elimination rate), Kin (AT production rate), Imax 
(maximum inhibition of AT production), and IC50 (RISC concentration for 50% of maximum 
effect) were estimated.

Figure 38. Schematic Representation of PopK-PD Model for AT Lowering 
 
Source: study-poh1116, Figure 2 
Table 136 summarizes all covariates that were investigated during the development of the PopK-
PD final model. The covariate model development started with a detailed exploratory covariate 
analysis that aimed to identify: (1) covariate distribution; (2) correlation between covariates; and 
(3) potential relationship between covariates and PopK-PD model parameters. The result of the 
exploratory covariate analysis was used as input to formally screen statistically significant 
covariates in the PopK-PD model. The formal covariate analysis was performed using a stepwise 
selection procedure. This procedure involves stepwise testing of linear and nonlinear 
relationships in a forward inclusion (ΔOFV of 6.63, p<0.01 for 1 degree of freedom [DF]) and 
backwards exclusion (ΔOFV of 10.8, p<0.001 for 1 DF) procedure. The covariate analysis 
identified statistically significant effects of bilirubin on RISC clearance and RACE on Kout, with 
AT protein being eliminated faster in Asians (i.e., Japan, Korea, China, Taiwan, India, and 
Malaysia) than the remaining category of race considered in the analysis dataset, i.e., Black, 
Caucasian, and “other” races (i.e., American Indian or Alaska Native, Native Hawaiian or other 
Pacific Islander, other multiple races, and not reported races).

Overall, the Applicant’s revised PopK-PD modeling and simulation analyses appear to be 
reasonable. Approximately 6% of subjects were predicted to discontinue treatment due to AT 
<15% with 20 mg Q2M, which was lower than the observed 11.7% (Table 138). This 
inconsistency between model prediction and observation might be due to the Applicant’s 
simulation approach, which did not consider the carryover effect due to the sustained AT activity 
inhibition from (1) the 80 mg QM regimen before the voluntary dosing pause; (2) AT-guided 
dose titration in the trial after the voluntary dosing pause (e.g., 50 mg Q2M de-escalate to 20 mg 
Q2M). To better evaluate the proposed dosing regimen and AT monitoring program, the 
reviewer performed independent PopK-PD model simulation analyses. The reviewer’s 
simulation matched with the observed individual AT profile relatively well (Figure 43 and 
Figure 44).

14.5.2. Evaluation for the Target AT Activity Range of 
15% to 35% 
Observed ABR records from 242 subjects in Studies EFC14768 (ITT), EFC14769 (ITT), and 
EFC15110 with fixed 80 mg QM were used to evaluate the correlation between AT activity and 
the efficacy endpoint ABR. Observations with the AT-based dosing regimen were not 
incorporated due to potential confounding by dose titration. The onset period for the 3 trials was 
the first 28 days. The efficacy period was Day 29 onwards until Month 9 for EFC14769, until 
Day 246 for EFC14769, and until Month 6 for EFC15110. Due to the limited duration of the 
onset period, a high number of zero-ABR values was observed. The average AT during the onset 
and efficacy periods was used to correlate with the observed ABR in the respective periods. The 
association plots, both with (Figure 45) and without (Figure 46) zero-ABR records, indicated that 
lower AT activity was potentially associated with lower ABR. However, it is unclear at which 
level of AT activity the ABR lowering plateaued, as the data did not provide sufficient 
information. Furthermore, the variability in follow-up duration among trials and between the 
onset and efficacy periods may have confounded the results.  
Figure 45. Observed ABR Over Averaged AT Activity (Zero ABR Included) 
 
Source: reviewer’s analysis

In addition to ALT and AST elevation, AT activity below 10% is associated with increased risk 
for thromboembolic events. Taking these results into consideration, the target AT activity range 
of 15% to 35% to balance efficacy and safety of fitusiran appears reasonable. 
14.5.3. Addition of a Lower 10 mg Q2M Dose for 
Patients With AT <15% on 20 mg Q2M 
In the clinical trials, 11.7% of subjects discontinued treatment due to AT activity <15% with the 
20 mg Q2M dosing regimen. However, the majority of subjects, who received 80 mg QM before 
the voluntary dosing pause, did not exhibit AT levels returning to baseline when starting the AT-
based dosing regimen after the dosing pause, which suggested a carryover effect of the 80 mg 
QM dosing regimen. As a result, the true percentage of subjects discontinuing treatment with the 
proposed dosing regimen without considering the carryover effect should be less than what was 
observed. The Applicant estimated that approximately 6% of the total virtual patient population 
would discontinue treatment due to AT levels below 15% with the 20 mg Q2M regimen. 
However, the Applicant’s simulation approach did not account for the carryover effect following 
dose titration. The reviewer conducted a PopK-PD simulation that considered the carryover 
effect due to changes in dosage. 
Reference ID: 5560526
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 

===== PAGE 247 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
226 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
The virtual patient population was generated by resampling each studied subject 10 times and 
incorporating sampled interindividual variability for corresponding PopK-PD parameters. Dose 
modification for each virtual patient was based on trough AT levels for down-titration and 
maximum (predose) AT levels for up-titration. A 6-month AT profile was simulated for each 
dose level after last dose modification and before next potential dose modification. 
The simulation indicated that 54.3% of the total virtual patient population remained on the 50 mg 
Q2M dosage regimen over time. Furthermore, 13.5% of the total virtual patients up-titrated to 
50 mg QM, while 32.3% down-titrated to 20 mg Q2M. Among the patients who were down-
titrated to 20 mg Q2M, 22% (representing 7% of the total virtual patient population) exhibited 
trough AT levels below 15%. To explore a lower dosage regimen that could retain these 7% of 
patients on fitusiran treatment, the reviewer simulated 10 mg Q2M and 15 mg Q2M using the 
virtual patients who had AT levels below 15% with the 20 mg Q2M regimen. The results 
suggested that 5.88% and 4.26% of the total virtual patient population could remain on treatment 
with the 10 mg Q2M and 15 mg Q2M regimens, respectively. Notably, neither regimen resulted 
in patients with AT levels exceeding 35%. Consequently, the review team recommended adding 
10 mg Q2M as a lower dose option for patients who would otherwise discontinue treatment due 
to AT levels below 15% with the 20 mg Q2M regimen. The patient distribution with the review-
team-proposed dosing regimen is shown in Figure 51.

14.5.4. Evaluation of AT Monitoring Program 
The Applicant proposed that AT activity be measured prior to initiation of fitusiran treatment and 
 If AT activity is 
 <15%, then a dose reduction is required, and the lower dose should be initiated 
3 months after the prior dose. If AT activity is >35% after 6 months, or if the patient has not 
achieved satisfactory bleed control, dose modification should be considered. In the clinical trials, 
subjects with more than one AT activity level <15% within a 12-month period must discontinue 
fitusiran dosing and may have the option to enroll into the lower dose cohort. Upon the first AT 
level <15%, the patient must have another AT sample drawn within 1 week of site receipt of the 
results. If this result is <15%, it was considered the second AT activity level <15%. Dose 
escalation in the clinical trial was based on 2 steady-state (Month 4, Month 5, and Month 6) AT 
activity levels >35%. To evaluate the appropriateness of the proposed AT monitoring program, 
the reviewer performed a PopK-PD simulation. 
Reference ID: 5560526
 
(b) (4)
(b) (4)

===== PAGE 249 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
228 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
For the Q2M regimen, AT inhibition reached its maximum approximately 4 weeks after each 
dose (Month 1, Month 3, and Month 5), whereas the predose AT activity was close to the 
minimum AT inhibition within each dosing interval (Figure 52). The dose de-escalation based on 
the AT activity measured predose every other month led to 14.2% of patients being down-titrated 
to 20 mg Q2M, whereas down-titration would occur in approximately 32.3% of patients if using 
the peak effect of fitusiran on AT activity measured at 4 weeks post each dose (Month 1, Month 
3, and Month 5) (Table 139). Considering the severe safety outcome associated with low AT 
activity, the review team recommended that dose de-escalation be based on AT activity 
measured at 4 weeks after each dose (Month 1, Month 3, and Month 5) rather than AT activity 
measured predose every other month. For the QM regimen, the fluctuation of AT activity 
between maximum and trough AT activity was minimal, and the AT activity measured at each 
month was close to the maximum AT inhibition (Figure 53). Additionally, patients on the QM 
dosing regimen had less concern for low levels of AT. As a result, the review-team-proposed AT 
monitoring program can be applied to a QM dosing regimen as well.

To evaluate the proposed dose escalation rule, the reviewer simulated three scenarios: (1) all 3 
predose AT >35%; (2) 2 out of 3 predose AT >35%; (3) at least 1 predose AT >35%. The 
proposed AT monitoring after 6 months is equivalent to the simulation scenario where 3 predose 
ATs are required to be >35% since the AT activity gradually decreases over time in each 
6-month period and AT at 6 months would be the maximum predose inhibition (Figure 53). All 
three scenarios had 0.46% of total virtual patients who had AT >35% after dose escalation. If the 
dose is escalated with only one predose AT >35%, then there is a potential risk to induce AT 
below 15% (Table 140). Based on the simulation results, the proposed dose escalation rule 
appears to be reasonable.

14.5.5. Evaluation of Covariate Effect of Race on AT 
In clinical trials, the majority (93.9%) of the subjects who discontinued the drug due to low AT 
levels were Asians. The covariate effect of race on AT activity was explored using PopK-PD 
simulation. The original PopK-PD model dataset consists of 41.9% of Caucasian (n=142), 1.5% 
of Black or African American (n=5), 53.4% of Asian (n=181), and 3.2% of other races (n=11). 
The virtual patients were resampled from the studied subjects while maintaining the same race 
Reference ID: 5560526
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
   
  
 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

===== PAGE 252 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
231 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
distribution as observed in the trial. The dose distribution stratified by race is shown in 
Figure 54. The simulation indicated Asian patients had the highest number of treatment 
discontinuations due to AT <15%. The percentage of Asian patients who discontinued treatment 
was larger than that of Caucasian patients. The percentage of discontinued patients was 
comparable between Asian and Race: Other groups. However, the limited number of patients in 
the Black and Race: Other groups makes it difficult to draw solid conclusions. The higher 
number of Asian patients who discontinued treatment in the trial was partially due to their larger 
representation in the studied population. The reviewer did not identify potential confounding 
factors (including body weight) that were imbalanced between patients who discontinued or 
remained on treatment (Figure 55). Since a lower 10 mg Q2M was recommended for all patients, 
no additional dose adjustment is needed based on race.

15. Study/Trial Design 
15.1. Study EFC14768 
Study Title 
ATLAS-INH: A Phase 3 Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of fitusiran in Patients with 
Hemophilia A or B, with Inhibitory Antibodies to Factor VIII or IX 
Study Dates 
Study initiation date: February 14, 2018 (date of first subject screened) 
Study completion: June 23, 2021 (last subject last visit) 
Reference ID: 5560526
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
 
 
 
 

===== PAGE 254 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
233 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Study Objectives 
Primary 
To evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with BPAs (bypassing 
agents), as determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes. 
Secondary 
1. To evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with BPAs, as 
determined by: 
a. The frequency of spontaneous bleeding episodes. 
b. The frequency of joint bleeding episodes. 
c. Health-related quality of life (HRQOL) in subjects ≥17 years of age 
2. To determine the frequency of bleeding episodes during the onset period (defined as the first 
28 days after the first dose of fitusiran, during which the target pharmacodynamic effect is 
reached). 
3. To determine the safety and tolerability of fitusiran. 
Study Endpoints 
Primary 
Annualized Bleeding Rate (ABR) for treated bleeds in the treatment period (defined in Section 
6.2, Table 14). 
Secondary 
1. ABR for treated bleeds in the efficacy period. 
2. Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate in the efficacy period (defined in Section 6.2, 
Table 14). 
3. Annualized treated joint bleeding rate in the efficacy period. 
4. Change in Hemophilia Quality of Life Questionnaire for Adults (Haem-A-QOL) physical 
health score and total score in the treatment period. 
Study Design 
The ATLAS-INH (EFC14768) study was a multicenter, multinational, randomized, open-label 
phase 3 study designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of fitusiran in male subjects aged ≥12 
years with hemophilia A or B and inhibitory antibodies to factor FVIII or factor FIX, who are not 
receiving prophylactic therapy. 
Eligible patients were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to either the fitusiran treatment arm, receiving 
80 mg administered subcutaneously (SC) as prophylaxis once monthly, with use of on-demand 
BPAs for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes; or the on-demand arm, receiving BPAs 
for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes. Randomization was stratified by the number of 
bleeding episodes in the 6 months prior to Screening (≤10 versus >10). All subjects were treated 
Reference ID: 5560526

===== PAGE 255 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
234 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
for a total of 9 months; subjects randomized to the fitusiran treatment arm received a total of 
9 SC injections of fitusiran. Because the full PD effect of fitusiran is not achieved until 
approximately 28 days after receiving the first dose, efficacy was assessed during the final 
8 months on study (Day 29 to Month 9). Scheduled assessments included monthly physical 
exams, triglyceride levels, AT activity levels, hematology labs, liver tests, and serum chemistry. 
Subjects from both the fitusiran and on-demand treatment arms who completed the study were 
eligible for participation in an open-label extension study. 
BPAs include recombinant factor VIIa, Factors VIII and IX, and activated prothrombin complex 
concentrates (aPCC). 
Breakthrough bleeding is defined as any occurrence of hemorrhage that requires administration 
of factor concentrates or BPA infusion, e.g., hemarthrosis, muscle, or mucosal bleeding. A 
spontaneous bleeding episode is a bleeding event that occurs for no apparent or known reason, 
particularly into the joints, muscles, and soft tissues. A joint bleeding episode is characterized by 
an unusual sensation in the joint (“aura”) in combination with: (1) increasing swelling or warmth 
over the skin over the joint; (2) increasing pain; or (3) progressive loss of range of motion or 
difficulty in using the limb as compared with baseline. A muscle bleed may be characterized by 
pain, swelling, and loss of movement over the affected muscle group. A target joint is defined as 
a joint where 3 or more spontaneous bleeding episodes in a single joint within a consecutive 
6-month period has occurred; where there have been ≤2 bleeding episodes in the joint within a 
consecutive 12-month period the joint is no longer considered a target joint. A traumatic 
bleeding episode is one that is caused by a known injury or trauma. Bleeding episodes sustained 
during sports and recreation were counted as traumatic bleeding episodes. 
Bleed Management Guidelines 
On-Demand Arm 
For subjects not receiving fitusiran (on-demand arm), bleeding management therapy with BPAs 
was to be managed based on the local standard practice for treating hemophilia patients with 
inhibitors, as routinely administered by the physician. Where clinical circumstances allowed, it 
was recommended that the subject contact the Investigator for all events suspected to be or 
characteristic of a bleeding episode. If adequate hemostasis did not occur after two doses of 
BPA, the subject was to contact the site for further instruction. If the subject felt the need to 
administer doses higher than the subject’s bleeding episode management plan recommended, or 
at a higher frequency, it was recommended that the subject contact the site. 
Fitusiran Treatment Arm 
Given the mechanism of action and PD profile of fitusiran, the BPA dose necessary to safely and 
effectively treat breakthrough bleeding episodes in subjects receiving fitusiran (fitusiran 
treatment arm) was lower than standardly prescribed (see Table 141 below). This was supported 
by data from the phase 1 study in which bleeding events were managed with factor or BPA, as 
well as additional modeling and ex vivo spiking data. 
Subjects on fitusiran were provided a written bleed management plan (as described below) with 
appropriate dosing of BPA for use during Day 1 through 7, as well as a bleed management plan 
with dosing for Day 8 and beyond. Thereafter the bleed management plan was reviewed and 
updated at monthly visits, and new written plans were provided to the subject if dosing changed. 
Reference ID: 5560526

===== PAGE 256 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
235 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Importantly, during the treatment period, subjects were not to use BPA or other hemostatic 
agents as prophylaxis for bleeding episode prevention, including doses related to anticipated 
hemostatic challenges such as physical activity. 
Day 1 to Day 7 
Subjects were to be instructed to call the site prior to administering factor or BPA. Subjects were 
to continue with their standard BPA regimens. 
Day 8 and Beyond 
1. Subject was to be instructed to call the study center to discuss symptoms to determine 
whether or not they were consistent with a bleeding event and to discuss the appropriate 
factor/BPA dose to use. This interaction between subject and Investigator is recommended 
prior to the administration of each dose of factor or BPA. Confirmation of bleeds at the study 
center prior to treatment may be considered. 
2. If a determination was made that symptoms required treatment, the recommended treatment 
algorithm for bleeding episodes is described below: 
a. A single dose could be administered according to the guidelines in Table 141. 
b. The subject was to be instructed to reevaluate symptoms in 24 hours for bleeds 
treated with FVIII, FIX or aPCC and in 2 to 3 hours for bleeds treated with rFVIIa. 
c. Administration of FIX Extended half-life should not be more frequent than every 5 to 
7 days. 
3. If a second dose (in the case of FVIII, FIX or aPCC) or a third dose (in the case of rFVIIa) 
was needed, the subject was to call the study center before dosing. 
a. Study staff were to consider evaluation and treatment of the subject at the study 
center and confirmation of bleeds when any repeated doses were needed. 
b. If more than two doses of FVIII, FIX, or aPCC, or three doses of rFVIIa were needed, 
the subject was to be seen at the study center within 48 to 72 hours. 
4. Doses were not to be administered at less than 24-hour intervals (except rFVIIa as indicated 
in Table 141). 
5. Doses were not to exceed the protocol maximum recommended dose indicated in Table 141. 
6. Consultation with the study Medical Monitor and Clinical Advisor was to be considered for 
clinical circumstances below, that may warrant AT replacement: 
a. Doses of factor or BPA higher than those recommended in Table 141 
b. Dosing of factor or BPA at decreased intervals than those recommended in Table 141 
c. Multiple or repeated doses of factor or BPA 
7. Antifibrinolytics were not to be used in combination with factor or BPA.

Perioperative Treatment Plan 
In subjects in both the on-demand and fitusiran treatment arms undergoing surgery, a written 
perioperative treatment plan was reviewed with the study Medical Monitor before conducting the 
procedure, unless clinical circumstances did not allow. In subjects on fitusiran, the perioperative 
treatment plans were developed using the same principles as bleed management. 
If a fitusiran dose was scheduled to occur on or in close proximity to the day of surgery, or 
anytime during the perioperative period, the dose should be withheld. The Perioperative 
Evaluation Period was defined as the day of the surgery through the final day on which 
supplemental hemostatic or antithrombotic treatments were administered as part of the 
perioperative treatment plan. Fitusiran dosing could be resumed at the next scheduled visit 
following the Perioperative Evaluation Period at the discretion of the Investigator. If multiple 
consecutive doses of fitusiran were withheld, the Investigator was to consult with the study 
Medical Monitor, who would determine if the subject could continue on study.

Study Design  
The ATLAS-A/B (EFC14769) study was a multicenter, multinational, randomized, open-label 
phase 3 study designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of fitusiran in male subjects aged ≥12 
years, with hemophilia A or B and without inhibitory antibodies to factor VIII (FVIII) or factor 
IX (FIX), who are not receiving prophylactic therapy. 
Eligible patients were to be randomized in a 2:1 ratio to either the fitusiran treatment arm 
receiving fitusiran 80 mg administered subcutaneously (SC) as prophylaxis once monthly, with 
use of on-demand factor concentrates or treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes, or the on-
demand arm with on-demand factor concentrates for treatment of breakthrough bleeding 
episodes. All patients will be treated for a total of 9 months; patients randomized to the fitusiran 
treatment arm will receive a total of 9 SC injections of fitusiran. Because the full PD effect of 
fitusiran is not achieved until approximately 28 days after receiving the first dose, efficacy will 
be assessed during the remaining 8 months on study (Day 29 to Month 9). Subjects from both the 
fitusiran and on-demand treatment arms who complete the study may be eligible for participation 
in an open-label extension study. 
Breakthrough bleeding definitions, Bleed Management Guidelines, and Perioperative Treatment 
Plan were the same as Study EFC14768 (See Section 15.1 above).

Study Endpoints 
Primary 
Incidence, severity, relatedness, and seriousness of AEs, and laboratory assessments. 
Secondary 
1. Annualized bleeding rate for treated bleeds in the treatment period. 
2. Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate in the treatment period. 
3. Annualized treated joint bleeding rate in the treatment period.

Study Design 
The ATLAS-OLE (LTE15174) study was a multicenter, multinational, open-label extension 
study of the long-term safety and efficacy of fitusiran in males ≥12 years of age with hemophilia 
A or B with or without inhibitory antibodies to FVIII or FIX. Subjects from all three parent 
studies (EFC14768, EFC14769, and ATLAS-PPX) were considered for enrollment.  
The study had 3 periods: 
 A screening period of up to 60 days (under certain circumstances, screening could be 
extended beyond 60 days following consultation with the Sponsor). 
 An open-label treatment period that could vary depending on the timing of subject’s restart 
after lifting of the dosing pause implemented in Q4 2020 (i.e., subjects were to be dosed up 
to 48 months after the restart): 
– Subjects that started fitusiran dosing in this study after lifting of the dosing pause were 
planned to have a maximum treatment duration of 48 months. 
– Subjects that had started fitusiran before the 2020 dosing pause were planned to have a 
maximum treatment duration of 76 months. 
 An approximately 6-month AT follow-up period after the last dose of fitusiran (note that 
some subjects may have undergone two 6-month AT follow-up periods, one at the end of the 
core study, i.e., before starting in the low dose cohort, and one at the completion of the whole 
study). 
On-demand factors or BPAs were utilized for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes. 
BPAs include recombinant factor VIIa, Factors VIII and IX, as well as activated prothrombin 
complex concentrates (aPCC). 
Scheduled assessments included monthly physical exams, hematology labs, and serum 
chemistry. Triglyceride levels, AT activity levels, and liver tests were done monthly for the first 
half of the study and then every 4 months. 
Breakthrough bleeding definitions, Bleed Management Guidelines, and Perioperative Treatment 
Plan were the same as Study EFC14768.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
The AT-DR initially consisted of a starting dose of 50 mg once every 2 months (Q2M) with 
options to escalate to 50 mg once monthly (QM) or 80 mg QM, or to de-escalate to 20 mg Q2M 
or 20 mg QM, based on an individual’s AT response. The AT-DR was initiated after a voluntary 
dosing pause in October 2020. In detail, the final AT-DR used in study LTE15174 stated that if 
at the starting dose of 50 mg Q2M, AT was <15% twice (per central lab), the study subjects 
would have to discontinue fitusiran administration at this dosing regimen and de-escalate to 
20 mg Q2M. If the steady state AT on at least two occasions was >35% (per central lab), the 
fitusiran dose was to be escalated to 50 mg QM (or 20 mg QM, respectively in the low dose 
cohort). If 50 mg QM also resulted in AT activity level >35%, then the subject could escalate to 
80 mg QM, provided that he had no prior history of central AT activity <15% more than one 
time during any prior exposure to fitusiran. Subjects previously exposed to fitusiran at a dose of 
80 mg monthly with no more than 1 AT activity level <15% at any time during fitusiran 
treatment (based on at least 3 months of prior AT measurements) had the option to remain on 
that dose. The Investigator could consider dose escalation on clinical grounds even if the subjects 
AT values were within 15 to 35% if bleed control was considered inadequate. 
Eligibility Criteria 
Inclusion Criteria 
1. Subject must be at least 12 years of age inclusive, at the time of signing the informed 
consent. 
2. Subjects with severe hemophilia A or B who have completed a phase 3 fitusiran clinical trial. 
3. Male - There are no contraceptive requirements for this study except where required by local 
regulations. 
4. Capable of giving signed informed consent which includes compliance with the requirements 
and restrictions listed in the ICF and in this protocol. In countries where legal age of majority 
is above 18 years, a specific ICF must also be signed by the subject’s legally authorized 
representative. 
Exclusion Criteria 
1. Completion of a surgical procedure within 14 days prior to screening, or currently receiving 
additional factor concentrate or BPA infusion for postoperative hemostasis. 
2. Current participation in immune tolerance induction therapy. 
3. Current use of factor concentrate or BPAs as regularly administered prophylaxis designed to 
prevent spontaneous bleeding episodes. However, subjects requiring factor concentrates or 
BPAs as prophylaxis during the study dosing pause period, per Investigator’s judgement in 
agreement with the study Medical Monitor, may be permitted to be enrolled. 
4. Use of compounds other than factor concentrates or BPAs for hemophilia treatment, 
including use of emicizumab (Hemlibra®) within 6 months prior to screening. 
5. Received an investigational drug or device, other than fitusiran, within 30 days of anticipated 
IMP administration or 5 half-lives of the IMP, whichever is longer. 
6. Current or prior participation in a gene therapy trial.

7. ALT and/or AST >1.5× upper limit of normal reference range (ULN) for subjects who were

Abbreviations: BPA, bypassing agent; BU, Bethesda units; CSR, clinical study report; max, maximum; min, minimum; Q1, first

Primary Endpoint Results 
Fitusiran demonstrated a reduced ABR for treated bleeds in the treatment period. The ABRs 
estimated based on negative binomial regression model in the treatment period were 2.0 (95% 
CI: 1.3, 3.1) for the fitusiran prophylaxis arm and 18.8 (95% CI: 11.5, 30.7) for the BPA on-
demand arm, corresponding to an 89.2% reduction in the bleeding rate associated with fitusiran 
prophylaxis during the treatment period (rate ratio of 0.11 [95% CI: 0.06, 0.21], p<0.0001).  
Secondary Endpoint Results 
There was a significant reduction in the spontaneous estimated ABR for treated bleeds in 
subjects treated with fitusiran (0.9 [95% CI: 0.5 to 1.6]) compared to those treated with BPA on 
demand (15.7 [95% CI: 9.3 to 26.5]), corresponding to a 94.4% reduction in the treated 
spontaneous bleeds associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the efficacy period (rate ratio of 
0.06 [95% CI: 0.03, 0.12], p<0.0001). 
The estimated ABR for treated bleeds in joints was 1.3 (95% CI: 0.8, 2.3) and 13.8 (95% CI: 8.0, 
23.8) in the fitusiran prophylaxis and BPA on demand arms, respectively, corresponding to a 
90.2% reduction in treated joint bleeds associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the efficacy 
period (rate ratio of 0.10 [95% CI: 0.05, 0.21], p<0.0001). 
Total scores (transformed) of Haem-A-QoL demonstrated overall improvements in perceived 
QoL in fitusiran prophylaxis arm, with reported LS mean (95% CI) -15.3 (-19.3, -11.2) and -0.4 
(-5.7, 4.8) reductions from baseline in the fitusiran prophylaxis and BPA on-demand arms, 
respectively. The observed LS mean difference was -14.9 (-21.4, -8.3) in favor of fitusiran 
(p<0.0001).

Primary Endpoint Results 
A reduced ABR for treated bleeds was demonstrated with fitusiran prophylaxis in the treatment 
period. The ABRs for treated bleeds estimated based on negative binomial regression model 
were 4.1 (95% CI: 3.1, 5.3) for the fitusiran prophylaxis arm and 31.4 (95% CI: 23.0, 43.0) for 
the factor on demand arm, corresponding to an 87.0% reduction in the bleeding rate associated 
with fitusiran prophylaxis during the treatment period (rate ratio of 0.13 [95% CI: 0.09, 0.19], 
p<0.0001).

There was a significant reduction in the spontaneous estimated ABR for treated bleeds in 
subjects treated with fitusiran (1.8 [95% CI: 1.2, 2.7]) compared to those treated on-demand 
(22.0 [95% CI: 14.2, 34.3]), corresponding to a 91.7% reduction in the treated spontaneous 
bleeds associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the efficacy period (rate ratio of 0.08 [95% 
CI: 0.05, 0.14], p<0.0001). 
The estimated ABR for treated bleeds in joints was 2.3 (95% CI: 1.6, 3.3) and 23.4 (95% CI: 
15.4, 35.7) in the fitusiran prophylaxis and factor on demand arms, respectively, corresponding 
to a 90.3% reduction in treated joint bleeds associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the 
efficacy period (rate ratio of 0.10 [95% CI: 0.06, 0.16], p<0.0001). 
The transformed total scores of Haem-A-QoL demonstrated overall improvements in perceived 
QoL in the fitusiran prophylaxis arm, with a reported LS mean (95% CI) of -9.7 (-12.5, -6.9), 
compared to the factor on demand arm LS mean of -2.6 (-6.3, 1.0). The LS mean difference was 
-7.1 (-11.2, -2.9) in favor of fitusiran (p=0.0011).

Pool C was not adequately powered for the analysis of ABR for treated bleeds and did not have

Pool C are considered descriptive in nature only. 
16.4.1. Design, Pool C  
ATLAS-PPX (EFC151110) was a multicenter, open-label study in male subjects aged ≥12 years 
with hemophilia A or B with or without inhibitory antibodies to FVIII or FIX, who switched to 
fitusiran from prior bypassing agent (BPA, Cohort A) or factor (Cohort B) prophylaxis. The 
study design is shown in Figure 56. 
The study had three periods. There was a 6-month factor or BPA prophylaxis period in which 
subjects continued their baseline, regularly scheduled prophylaxis regimen with factor 
concentrates or BPAs. Next, a 1-month onset period in which subjects received their first dose of 
fitusiran while continuing their factor or BPA prophylaxis for up to 7 days. Then, a 6-month 
fitusiran efficacy period in which subjects received fitusiran as prophylaxis. Together, the 
1-month onset period and the 6-month fitusiran efficacy period constitute the fitusiran treatment 
period. Throughout the study, subjects may have received on-demand treatment for breakthrough 
bleeding episodes with factors or BPAs, as appropriate. Details on management of breakthrough 
bleeds are described in Section 15. All subjects were treated for 9 months and, upon completion, 
were eligible for participation in an open-label extension study.

The Study design for Study ATLAS-OLE (LTE15174) is described in Section 15.3. 
16.4.2. Objectives and Endpoints, Pool C  
Objectives 
The primary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran treatment at the revised AT-DR 
compared to standard-of-care (SOC) prophylaxis as determined by the frequency of bleeding 
episodes. 
The major secondary objectives: 
 To characterize the following while receiving fitusiran treatment, relative to receiving factor 
or BPA prophylaxis: 
– The frequency of spontaneous bleeding episodes. 
– The frequency of joint bleeding episodes. 
– Health-related quality of life (HRQOL) in subjects ≥17 years of age. 
 To characterize the frequency of bleeding episodes during the onset and treatment periods in 
subjects receiving fitusiran. 
Endpoints 
The primary efficacy endpoint was annualized bleeding rate (ABR) for treated bleeds in the 
fitusiran primary efficacy period compared to the control period (Table 14). 
The major secondary endpoints are listed below: 
1. Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate (AsBR). 
2. Annualized treated joint bleeding rate (AjBR). 
3. Change in Haemophilia Quality of Life Questionnaire for Adults (Haem-A-QoL) Physical 
Health domain score.

16.4.3. Eligibility Criteria, Pool C 
Eligibility criteria for Study EFC151110 are described below. Study LTE15174 included 
subjects who completed a previous parent phase 3 clinical study of fitusiran and had central AT 
activity level ≥22% (prior to dosing). 
ATLAS-PPX (EFC151110) 
Key Inclusion Criteria 
1. Males ≥12 years of age. 
2. Severe hemophilia A or B as evidenced by: 
a. A central laboratory measurement at screening or documented medical record evidence 
of FVIII <1% or FIX level ≤2%. 
3. A minimum of 2 bleeding episodes requiring BPA treatment within the last 6 months prior to 
Screening for patients with inhibitory antibodies to Factor VIII or Factor IX (Cohort A). A 
minimum of 1 bleeding episode requiring factor treatment within the last 12 months prior to 
Screening for patients without inhibitory antibodies to Factor VIII or Factor IX (Cohort B). 
4. Must meet either the definition of inhibitor or noninhibitor patient as below: 
a. Inhibitor 
i. 
Use of BPAs for prophylaxis and for any bleeding episodes for at least the last 6 
months prior to Screening, and meet one of the following Nijmegen-modified 
Bethesda assay results criteria: 
 Inhibitor titer of ≥0.6 BU/mL at Screening, or 
 Inhibitor titer of <0.6 BU/mL at Screening with medical record evidence of 2 
consecutive titers ≥0.6 BU/mL, or 
 Inhibitor titer of <0.6 BU/mL at Screening with medical record evidence of 
anamnestic response 
ii. 
The subgroup of patients in Cohort A patients must additionally meet the 
following criteria to be eligible to start treatment with fitusiran directly after the 
screening period: 
 Hemophilia B with inhibitory antibody to Factor IX as defined above 
 Not responding adequately to BPA treatment (historical ABR ≥20) prior to 
enrollment 
 In the opinion of the Investigator, with approval of Sponsor Medical Monitor, 
6-month BPA prophylaxis period should be omitted.

16.4.4. Statistical Analysis Plan, Pool C 
Analysis Population 
The following analysis sets were defined for the statistical analysis: 
Full analysis set for Pool C of Study EFC15110 (ATLAS-PPX)/Study LTE15174 (ATLAS-
OLE): 
 For treatment period: AT-DR fitusiran treatment data were generated from both the extension 
study LTE15174 and study EFC15110. 
 For control period: SOC prophylaxis control data were generated from the control period of 
the original switching study EFC15110. 
Analysis of the Primary Endpoint 
The primary efficacy endpoint was annualized bleeding rate (ABR) for treated bleeds. 
The primary endpoint was analyzed using a repeated measures negative binomial model. The 
variable was the number of bleeding episodes. The study period for Switching in Pool C 
(primary efficacy period or control period) was treated as fixed effects. The robust sandwich 
covariance matrix constructed to account for within-subject dependence was considered for 
Switching Pool C. The logarithm of the duration (in years) that each subject spent in each study 
period matching the bleeding episode data being analyzed was included as an offset variable to 
account for unequal follow-up time due to early withdrawal, surgery, etc.  
Neither the parent study EFC15110 nor Pool C was adequately powered for the analysis of ABR 
for treated bleeds. Pool C did not have any formal hypothesis to test either superiority or 
noninferiority of fitusiran prophylaxis compared to prior SOC prophylaxis. 
Reference ID: 5560526

===== PAGE 272 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
251 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Analysis of Major Secondary Endpoints 
Annualized Treated Spontaneous Bleeding Rate 
The same analysis method for the primary endpoint was used for this key secondary endpoint. 
Annualized Treated Joint Bleeding Rate 
The same analysis method for the primary endpoint was used for this key secondary endpoint. 
Change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health Domain Score 
Change from baseline in Haem-A-QoL physical health score (in subjects ≥17 years of age) in the 
control period and primary efficacy period were analyzed using a mixed model for repeated 
measure analysis (MMRM) with robust sandwich covariance matrix, with fixed effects of study 
period (factor or BPA prophylaxis period and fitusiran treatment period) and baseline Haem-A-
QoL Physical Health domain score for each period. 
Multiple Testing Strategy 
No multiplicity control was provided. 
Handling of Intercurrent Events and Missing Data  
Handling of intercurrent events and missing data are the same as described for Pool A in Section 
6.2.1.4. 
16.4.5. Results of Analyses, Pool C 
Subject Disposition 
The population for Pool C was comprised of subjects in Study LTE15174 and Study EFC15110 
who switched from SOC prophylaxis to fitusiran prophylaxis. The full analysis set for the SOC 
prophylaxis arm is comprised of all EFC15110 enrolled subjects who received factor or BPA 
prophylaxis in Study EFC15110 and any dose of fitusiran. The fitusiran prophylaxis arm is 
comprised of all subjects who received at least one dose of fitusiran. Fifty-nine (85.5%) subjects 
were enrolled and entered the fitusiran prophylaxis arm in the treatment period (dose resumption 
Day 1 to Day 364). There were 54 subjects who received at least one dose of fitusiran who 
completed the study period and 5 subjects who received at least one dose of fitusiran who did not 
complete the study period. Of these 5 subjects, one (1.4%) discontinued the study because of 
adverse events, 2 (2.9%) withdrew consent, and 2 (2.9%) were discontinued because of more 
than 1 AT measurement <15% (Table 146).
