Analysis of the Primary Endpoint 
Table 148 summarizes the annualized bleeding rates (ABR) for treated bleeds in the fitusiran 
prophylaxis period compared to the SOC prophylaxis period as determined by the Applicant. The 
estimated ABR was 7.5 (95% CI: 5.6, 10.1) for the SOC prophylaxis period and 5.7 (95% CI: 
3.9, 8.2) for the fitusiran prophylaxis (AT-DR) period. The rate ratio (AT-DR prophylaxis/SOC 
prophylaxis) was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.47, 1.2). There were no formal hypotheses preplanned and the 
results were exploratory only and should be interpreted with caution. However, the results 
observed appear descriptively to be similar with both types of prophylaxis. For exploratory 
purposes, the results of the subjects on prior prophylaxis with a fixed dose of fitusiran in study

The FDA review team obtained slightly different efficacy results than described above. The FDA 
review team derived an estimated ABR for treated bleeds of 5.8 (4.0, 8.4) in the fitusiran period 
and 7.5 (5.6, 10.1) in the SOC prophylaxis period, with a corresponding rate ratio of 0.77 with a 
nominal p-value of 0.26. The team sent an information request (IR) on December 5, 2024, to 
request clarification, and the Applicant responded that the discrepancy in the estimates was 
numerically small and the overall conclusion remains unchanged. The difference appears to be 
related to a technical issue in the operating system of running SAS programs. 
Subgroup analyses were conducted for the primary endpoint by hemophilia types (i.e., HA or 
HB) and by different inhibitor cohorts (i.e., with or without inhibitors). In the Type C Meeting, 
on July 26, 2018, the Division recommended formal statistical testing for study EFC15110 in 
order to determine the treatment effect of fitusiran in subjects switched from factor or BPA 
prophylaxis. In addition, the Division recommended to include sufficient numbers of subjects 
with and without inhibitors, in order for efficacy to be assessed in both populations. However, 
neither study EFC15110 nor Pool C included formal statistical testing for efficacy endpoints, 
including the primary efficacy endpoint ABR. The ABRs in different inhibitor cohorts were not 
prespecified endpoints. In the final statistical analysis plan for study EFC15110, the 
determination of the sample size for study EFC15110 was based on clinical considerations rather 
than a power calculation, and the Applicant stated that the p-value should be interpreted with 
caution. 
Although the final clinical study reports for study EFC15110 and Pool C presented superiority 
testing results for the primary efficacy endpoint ABR, without adequately powered formal 
statistical testing to detect differences, the analyses are considered exploratory. The Agency 
notified the Applicant in a Type C Meeting on September 18, 2023, that the results of this study 
would be descriptive, as there was no prespecified decision rule. Given the exploratory nature of 
this analysis, results from Pool C will not be included in labeling.

16.5. Patient-Reported Hemophilia-Related 
Symptoms and Physical Functioning for Pools 
A and B 
The patient-reported outcome (PRO) endpoint of change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health 
domain score from baseline was included by the Applicant in the statistical testing hierarchy as 
the third key secondary endpoint for Pools A and B 
. 
Analysis of this endpoint for Pool A failed to reach statistical significance, whereas in Pool B, 
this endpoint did reach statistical significance.  
The Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain is a reasonable option to assess patient-reported 
hemophilia signs/symptoms (swellings and joint pain) and physical functioning
. However, in a Type C 
Guidance meeting on September 18, 2023, the Agency raised the concerns that the open-label 
design limits interpretation of the PRO, as a subject’s knowledge of treatment assignment may 
lead to systematic over- or underestimation of the treatment effect, the magnitude of which is 
unknown. In addition, the Agency informed the Applicant that the 2-year difference between the 
AT-DR in the long-term extension study also posed challenges in interpretation of the PRO 
endpoint. 
Table 149 briefly summarizes the primary efficacy analysis results for the PRO endpoint change 
in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score from baseline in Pools A and B. Please refer to 
Section 6.2 for further details.

The following concerns limit the interpretability of the PRO data comparing the fitusiran AT-DR 
treatment with the control treatment: 
Baseline Data 
The AT-based dosing regimen does not have any true baseline values (i.e., the transition from 
fitusiran 80 mg to the AT-based dosing regimen consisted of a variable period that was 
individualized); as such, it is difficult to compare the data between control and the fitusiran 
Reference ID: 5560526
(b) (4)
(b) (4)
(b) (4)

===== PAGE 276 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
255 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
AT-based dose regimen from an efficacy standpoint. Further, there is no matching control for the 
AT-based dosed regimen in the primary efficacy period. 
Open-Label Design 
The interpretability of PRO data is limited because subjects’ knowledge of treatment assignment 
may lead to systematic overestimation or underestimation of treatment effect, the magnitude of 
which is unknown. In settings where blinding is not feasible, or there is high likelihood of 
inadvertent unblinding due to toxicity, lack of blinding will need to be overcome by 
demonstrating a large and durable magnitude of effect in the setting of strict adherence to a 
carefully conducted clinical trial. Pool A failed to achieve statistical significance, and Pool B 
only achieved a marginally significant p-value for the superiority test comparing fitusiran 
AT-DR treatment with control treatment for the PRO endpoints. The effect sizes in Pools A and 
B may not be able to account for the bias introduced by the open-label design of the study for 
PRO endpoints. 
Time Lag in the Pooled Analysis 
In Pools A and B, the calculation of change from baseline in the fitusiran arm started from Day 1 
of the parent studies with the original fitusiran 80 mg QM dose regimen treatment to the end of 
the primary efficacy period with the fitusiran AT-DR treatment, which involves the period of 
both the fitusiran 80 mg QM original dosing regimen treatment and the fitusiran AT-DR 
treatment. The time lag introduces further uncertainty on the estimated treatment effect for the 
PRO endpoints, with the much longer period of the subjects on fitusiran treatment (i.e., over 21 
months, including at least 9 months on fitusiran 80 mg QM plus 12 months on fitusiran AT-DR 
compared to 9 months for the control treatment) and the higher rate of subject dropout due to the 
long treatment period for the fitusiran treatment. Please refer to Figure 57 below for the length of 
each period. 
Figure 57. Graphic Illustration of Treatment Periods for Subjects in Pool A and Pool B 
 
Source: DCOA Reviewer’s sketch  
Robustness of the Primary Analysis Results 
Table 150 summarizes the missing pattern for the PRO endpoint in Pools A and B. The missing 
data rate in the fitusiran AT-DR treatment arm is considerably higher than the on-demand control 
arm.

In the primary analysis for the PRO endpoint change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain 
score from baseline using the MMRM approach, no imputation of missing data in the control 
period was performed for subjects who withdrew in the control period; unobserved data for the 
primary efficacy period were imputed via multiple imputation method with missing at random 
(MAR) assumption based on observed data during the primary efficacy period. To evaluate the 
robustness of the primary analysis result against the approach to handle missing data, the FDA 
statistical review team requested several sensitivity analyses via information request dated 
November 22, 2024, for Pools A and B, including: 
1. The result of multiple imputation can vary due to reasons such as the selection of imputation 
method/model under MAR assumption and the selection of seed for the imputation. 
Furthermore, a multiple imputation for missing data under MAR assumption is not necessary 
for MMRM analysis since this model already imputes missing data under the MAR 
assumption. A sensitivity analysis using MMRM approach was conducted based only on the 
observed data without any imputation for the unobserved data for Pools A and B (Table 151).

Pool A failed to achieve statistically significant results in the primary analysis, nor did it achieve 
nominal significant results in the sensitivity analyses. None of the above sensitivity analyses for 
Pool B achieved the same conclusion (i.e., nominal p-value<0.05) as the primary analysis results, 
indicating that the primary analysis result for Pool B is not robust against the approach to handle 
missing data. The p-value for the primary analysis should be interpreted with caution. 
17. Clinical Safety 
17.1. Safety Analysis of Pooled Trials EFC14768 
and EFC14769 
Trials EFC14768 and EFC14769 are open-label controlled trials that randomized subjects with 
hemophilia A and B with and without inhibitors, respectively, to receive fitusiran 80 mg SQ QM 
or on-demand therapy over a 9-month treatment period. High-level safety data are provided 
below. For dose-related adverse reactions, the safety profile of 80 mg QM could differ from that 
of the AT-DR, as the 80 mg QM regimen is a higher dose. However, these analyses are useful 
because they provide randomized, controlled comparisons and could potentially identify safety 
signals that were not seen in the uncontrolled long-term extension study.

Serious Adverse Events 
Table 155 shows the SAEs by system organ class and PT. Only fitusiran-treated patients had 
SAEs reported in the hepatobiliary disorders SOC (PTs biliary colic, cholecystitis acute, 
cholecystitis chronic, cholecystitis and cholelithiasis), vascular SOC (PTs thrombosis and 
subclavian vein thrombosis), and spinal vascular disorder. 
Overall, the most prevalent SAEs occurring only in the fitusiran arms in studies EFC14768 and 
EFC14769, were hepatobiliary disorders (biliary colic, cholecystitis acute, cholecystitis chronic). 
This mirrored the most prevalent SAEs associated with fitusiran use in the AT-DR open-label 
study, cholecystitis (Table 41, Section 7.5.2.1).

Most Common TEAEs 
The most common TEAEs by FMQ are shown in Table 157. 
The most common preferred term (PT), hepatic injury, occurred nearly exclusively in the 
fitusiran treatment arms compared to the on-demand arms, and in each study the risk difference 
95% confidence interval did not cross zero. Cholecystitis and dyspepsia were prevalent PTs 
reported only in the fitusiran treatment arms. Abdominal pain was also mostly reported in the 
fitusiran treatment arms. Dyspepsia and abdominal pain will be included as clinically relevant 
adverse reactions.

Liver Assessment 
Liver enzyme elevations were higher on the 80 mg QM dose regimen, compared to the AT-DR. 
This is discussed in the safety review issue for hepatotoxicity (see Section 7.6.3 for further 
discussion).

Figure 58. Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Pooled 
Trials EFC14768 and EFC14769 
 
 
Source: adlb.xpt; Software: R 
Each data point represents a subject plotted by their maximum ALT or AST versus their maximum total bilirubin values in the 
postbaseline period. 
A potential Hy's Law case was defined as having any postbaseline total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2× ULN after a postbaseline 
ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3× ULN. Those subjects who meet total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2× ULN criteria within 30 
days of the ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3× ULN criteria are circled in red.  
The within 30 days analysis window rule does not apply to cholestasis and temple’s corollary cases.  
All subjects with at least one postbaseline ALT or AST, bilirubin and ULN are plotted.  
In addition to central laboratory data, local laboratory data may be included in the analysis, if applicable. 
For number of subjects in each quadrant, see the table “Subjects in Each Quadrant for Potential Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver 
Injury Screening Plot …” and the listing “Listing of Subjects in Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening….”

268 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
17.2. Safety Analysis of Pooled Trials EFC14768 
and EFC14769 On-Demand Controls and 
LTE15174 AT-DR Treatment Phase Subjects 
Originally Randomized to Fitusiran in Parent 
Study EFC14768 and EFC14769 
To further assess potential treatment effects of fitusiran AT-DR, an exploratory analysis 
compared subjects receiving the AT-DR during LTE15174 (N=103; 35 inhibitor and 68 
noninhibitor) with the subjects randomized to the on-demand treatment arm (N=59; 19 inhibitor 
and 40 noninhibitor) from studies EFC14768 and EFC14769. For this analysis, we only included 
AT-DR subjects who had originally been randomized to the fitusiran treatment arm in studies 
EFC14768 and EFC14769. An important limitation of this analysis is that patients on the AT-DR 
who tolerated the higher dose of fitusiran in the parent studies and then continued into the long-
term extension study may not reflect what would occur in patients who are treatment-naïve to 
fitusiran AT-DR. 
Exposure Data 
Table 159 shows the duration of exposure to fitusiran AT-DR in Study LTE15174 and exposure 
to the on-demand treatment in EFC14768 and EFC14769. The mean duration of treatment of 
107.2 weeks in the fitusiran AT-DR cohort is longer than the mean duration of 35.4 weeks for 
the on-demand cohort. In addition, as this analysis is adhering to the original randomization, 
there are more subjects in the fitusiran AT-DR cohort due to the original 2:1 randomization 
scheme.

Overview of Adverse Events 
Table 160 shows the overview of adverse events. Given the differences in exposure, the 
frequencies are reported in an exposure-adjusted incidence rate (EAIR, per 100 person-years). 
SAEs were lower in the fitusiran treatment cohort than in the on-demand cohort. Table 161 
shows subjects with SAEs by SOC and PT. Conclusions are limited by the low numbers of 
events.

Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as any AE with onset or ongoing after fitusiran dose restart and before the data cut off (June 14, 2023) for NDA for the fitusiran group. 
However, AEs were not considered treatment-emergent if they occurred during the follow-up after a subject’s AT value was 60% or greater. For the on-demand group, AEs were 
defined as treatment-emergent if they occurred after the Day 1 visit during studies EFC14768 and EFC14769. 
Duration is up to 128 weeks for the fitusiran group and 36 weeks for the on-demand group.  
Events occurred during major surgery periods are excluded. 
The revised regimen period is from dose restart Day 1 to data cut off for interim lock and follow-up AT back to 60% whichever comes first. 
Person-years for the AT-DR group were calculated as the total duration starting from the first dose of AT-DR until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an 
event, or the data cutoff date for subjects who did not. However, for subjects who discontinued earlier, the follow-up period was considered completed once the AT recovered to 60%. 
Person-years for the on-demand group were calculated as the total duration starting from the Day 1 visit until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an event, 
or the last day in the study for subjects who did not. 
Risk difference (with 95% confidence interval) is shown between total treatment and comparator. 
Severity as assessed by the investigator.  
Note that differences are sometimes observed between Table 3 and Table 5 because these tables are derived from different datasets and sometimes differ in definitions or how cases 
are counted. 
Asterisk (*) indicates that 95% confidence interval excludes zero.

Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as any AE with onset or ongoing after fitusiran dose restart and before the data cut off (June 14, 2023) for NDA for the fitusiran group. 
However, AEs were not considered treatment-emergent if they occurred during the follow-up after a subject’s AT value was 60% or greater. For the on-demand group, AEs were 
defined as treatment-emergent if they occurred after the Day 1 visit during studies EFC14768 and EFC14769. 
Serious adverse events defined as any untoward medical occurrence that at any dose results in death, is life-threatening, requires hospitalization or prolongation of existing 
hospitalization, results in persistent incapacity or substantial disruption of the ability to conduct normal life functions, or is a congenital anomaly or birth defect. 
Duration is up to 128 weeks for the fitusiran group and 36 weeks for the on-demand group.  
Events occurred during major surgery periods are excluded. 
The revised regimen period is from dose restart Day 1 to data cut off for interim lock and follow-up AT back to 60% whichever comes first. 
Person-years for the AT-DR group were calculated as the total duration starting from the first dose of AT-DR until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an 
event, or the data cutoff date for subjects who did not. However, for subjects who discontinued earlier, the follow-up period was considered completed once the AT recovered to 60%. 
Person-years for the on-demand group were calculated as the total duration starting from the Day 1 visit until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an event, 
or the last day in the study for subjects who did not. 
Risk difference (with 95% confidence interval) is shown between total treatment and comparator.

273 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Most Common Adverse Events 
Table 162 shows the adverse events by SOC and FDA Medical Query. Many AEs were reported in only 1-2 subjects, limiting 
conclusions. More frequently reported AEs of cough, bronchospasm, and rash were reported almost exclusively with fitusiran 
treatment compared to on-demand treatment, similar to the distribution of these AEs in the randomized controlled trials. The PT viral 
infection was reported at a higher rate with fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment, like in the randomized controlled trials, 
although bacterial infection and nasopharyngitis rates were lower with fitusiran, in contrast to that seen in the randomized trials.

Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as any AE with onset or ongoing after fitusiran dose restart and before the data cut off (June 14, 2023) for NDA for the fitusiran group. 
However, AEs were not considered treatment-emergent if they occurred during the follow-up after a subject’s AT value was 60% or greater. For the on-demand group, AEs were 
defined as treatment-emergent if they occurred after the Day 1 visit during studies EFC14768 and EFC14769. 
Duration is up to 128 weeks for the fitusiran group and 36 weeks for the on-demand group.  
Events occurred during major surgery periods are excluded. 
The revised regimen period is from dose restart Day 1 to data cut off for interim lock and follow-up AT back to 60% whichever comes first. 
Person-years for the AT-DR group were calculated as the total duration starting from the first dose of AT-DR until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an 
event, or the data cutoff date for subjects who did not. However, for subjects who discontinued earlier, the follow-up period was considered completed once the AT recovered to 60%. 
Person-years for the on-demand group were calculated as the total duration starting from the Day 1 visit until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an event, 
or the last day in the study for subjects who did not. 
Risk difference (with 95% confidence interval) is shown between total treatment and comparator. 
Each FMQ is aligned to a single SOC based on clinical judgment. However, please be aware that some FMQs may contain PTs from more than one SOC.  
Some preferred terms are not included in any FDA medical query. Those preferred terms are not shown or counted in this table.  
Asterisk (*) indicates that 95% confidence interval excludes zero. 
Abbreviations: AE, adverse event; AT, antithrombin; AT-DR, antithrombin-based dose regimen; CI, confidence interval; EAIR, exposure-adjusted incidence rate (per 100 person-
years); FMQ, FDA medical query; N, number of subjects in treatment arm; n, number of subjects with at least one event; PT, preferred term; PY, person-years (total exposure); 
py, person-years (at risk); SOC, system organ class; TIA, transient ischemic attack 
Liver Assessment 
While less compared to the 80 mg QM dose regimen, liver enzyme abnormalities also occurred at higher rates in the AT-DR arm 
compared to on-demand. This issue is discussed in Section 7.6.3.

17.3. Thrombotic Event Narratives 
Subject narratives for thrombotic events are summarized below. 
EFC14768, Fitusiran 80 mg QM 
1. Subject 
 was a 17-year-old with hemophilia B with inhibitors and a 
relevant history of pancreatic insufficiency, malabsorption syndrome, prior central catheter 
infections, and prior deep venous thrombosis of the right upper extremity, who presented on 
Day 63 with an SAE of deep vein thrombosis, subclavian vein thrombosis and superficial 
thrombophlebitis (PTs). The subject had a recent catheter-device-related infection that 
occurred on Day 57, which required hospitalization, removal of both an indwelling port and a 
right-sided PICC line, and extended intravenous antibiotic therapy. The thrombotic event 
occurred during the hospitalization for the catheter-device infection and extended the 
hospitalization. The thrombotic event was in the anatomical location of the recent PICC 
catheter, and imaging showed occlusive thrombus in the right brachial vein, chronic 
nonocclusive thrombus in the right subclavian vein, and right axillary occlusive thrombus. 
Fitusiran was continued and the subject completed the study treatment period, with the last 
dose of fitusiran 80 mg SC QM occurring on Day 239. The clinical review team considers 
that while there were many ongoing risk factors for DVT, fitusiran likely contributed to the 
risk of this event. 
2. Subject 
 was a 19-year-old who experienced an SAE of thrombosis 
(suspected spinal vessel thrombosis) on Day 204. The subject had a history of hemophilia 
A with inhibitors. He presented with right hand and bilateral thigh numbness, which 
progressed to right hand weakness and bilateral leg weakness, requiring aids for ambulation. 
On Day 228, MRI of the lumbar spine showed nonspecific C6/C7 spinal cord hyperintensity 
with mild cord expansion, but did not show explicit evidence of hematoma, acute spinal 
infarct or spinal artery thrombosis, or spinal tumor. A whole-spine MRI showed a focal area 
of T2 hyperintensity within the spinal cord, centered at C7, consistent with an area of 
myelopathy. Differential diagnosis included cord ischemia or demyelination. An angiogram 
was not performed. The subject was treated with antithrombin concentrates on Days 255 and 
256, and then on Days 302 to 306. Neurological findings improved gradually over 2 to 3 
months following administration of antithrombin concentrates. Fitusiran was stopped, with 
last dose given (80 mg QM) on Day 204. The review team considers that the most consistent 
imaging findings were of spinal cord ischemia, and while the nature of the underlying spinal 
cord process (whether primary thrombotic, infectious or inflammatory) is unclear, vascular 
insufficiency appears to have played a role. Features of this case suggesting that fitusiran was 
related were that the symptoms resolved over time without other treatment than fitusiran 
withdrawal and as antithrombin levels recovered to the normal range, and that vascular 
insufficiency due to thrombosis is compatible with fitusiran’s mechanism of action. 
Reference ID: 5560526
(b) (6)

EFC15110, Fitusiran Dose 80 mg QM 
1. Subject 
 was a 36-year-old male with a history of hemophilia A without 
inhibitors, prior deep vein thrombosis, obesity, hypertension, hyperlipidemia, tobacco use, 
and diabetes mellitus who experienced the SAE of cerebrovascular accident. On Day 160 
of fitusiran dosing, the subject presented with left-sided paralysis, and imaging showed 
lesions in the right basal ganglia and corona radiata. A CT angiogram showed occlusion of 
the M1 division of the right middle cerebral artery. The last dose of fitusiran (80 mg QM) 
was given on Day 146, and fitusiran was discontinued. The clinical review team recognizes 
that the subject had many vascular risk factors, but fitusiran contribution is likely. 
2. Subject 
 was a 56-year-old male with HA with inhibitors and a 
history of hypertension and renal calculi, who experienced the SAE of thrombosis, papilla 
of left eye on Day 257. He presented on Day 247 with 95% sudden loss of vision in his left 
eye, associated with fatigue, headache, and dizziness. MRI did not show vascular or other 
lesions. Carotid ultrasounds showed a calcified plaque at the right carotid bulb. Eight days 
later, the subject presented to a neurologist and exam confirmed severe loss of vision and 
papilledema of the left eye. Prednisolone was started. Three days later, cranial MR 
venography did not reveal cerebral sinus thrombosis. The neurologist concluded that the 
episode was due to capillary occlusion of the left papilla, on Day 257. The subject received 
the last dose of fitusiran on Day 170 (80 mg QM) and remains with vision loss. The review 
team considers that this acute painful visual loss associated with papilledema likely 
represents a microvascular arterial or venous thrombotic event, and given the mechanism of 
action, fitusiran likely contributed. 
LTE14762: Fitusiran Dose 80 mg QM 
Subject 
 was a 25-year-old White male with hemophilia A without 
inhibitors who experienced cerebral venous sinus thrombosis that resulted in a fatal outcome 
(originally reported as PT subarachnoid hemorrhage). The subject narrative can be found in 
Section 7.5.2.2.  
LTE15174: Fitusiran dose 80 mg QM 
Subject LTE15174 
 was a 62-year-old with hemophilia A without inhibitors 
with a past medical history of hepatitis C, prostate cancer post radical prostatectomy 3 years 
earlier, HIV infection, and bronchiectasis, who experienced a cerebral artery embolism on Day 
436. The subject presented with aphasia. MRI showed numerous small infarcts involving the left 
middle cerebral artery territory with absent flow in the M2 branch. Fitusiran was discontinued. 
The subject improved but did not fully recover neurologic deficits. The review team considers 
that while the subject had cardiovascular risk factors of HIV, prior cancer, and bronchiectasis, 
fitusiran, due to its mechanism of action, is a contributing factor to the cerebral artery aneurysm. 
Reference ID: 5560526

LTE15174: Fitusiran Dose 50 mg Q2M 
1. Subject 
 was a 13-year-old male with hemophilia B with inhibitors who 
experienced a serious TEAE of venous thrombosis. Of note, per-protocol TEAEs that 
occurred during the perioperative period of a major surgery were not considered SAEs. The 
subject had a prior history of deep venous thrombosis of the vena cava and subclavian vein 
thrombosis, thrombectomy, and venous angioplasty. On Day 687, he presented with pain in 
the left upper extremity, and ultrasound of the extremity revealed venous thrombosis of the 
left axillary, brachial and basilic veins. The event of venous thrombosis was considered 
serious because it required another admission to the hospital. Of note, the subject had 
recently undergone right inguinal hernia repair (Day 682), which was complicated by an 
incision site hematoma that required incision and drainage and drain placement. The subject 
was treated with argatroban and by Day 690 recovered from both the venous thrombosis and 
incision hematoma revision and was discharged. The subject continued to receive fitusiran. 
The clinical review team’s assessment is that while the subject was at increased risk of 
venous thrombosis due to recent hernia surgery with incisional hematoma and revision, and 
prior venous thrombotic events in the same anatomic region, fitusiran likely increased venous 
thrombosis risk. 
2. Subject 
 was a 40-year-old male with HA who permanently discontinued 
fitusiran due to the AE of deep vein thrombosis postoperative. See subject narrative in 
Section 7.6.1. 
3. Subject 
 permanently discontinued after the AE of cerebral infarction. 
See subject narrative in Section 7.6.1 
4. Subject 
 was a 54-year-old male with hemophilia who experienced an 
SAE of embolic stroke. The subject narrative can be found in Section 7.6.1. 
17.4. Acute Gallbladder Events 
Table 164 shows the demographic features, fitusiran dosing, and gallbladder AEs in the 
12 subjects who underwent cholecystectomy in the fitusiran development program. Most 
cholecystectomies were performed while subjects were receiving non-AT-DR dose regimens, 
although one subject had transitioned to AT-DR dosing 589 days before cholecystectomy, and 
another developed cholangitis and pancreatitis while on AT-DR dosing 889 days after 
cholecystectomy.

submitted to the Agency for assessment appears acceptable in support of the proposed indication. 
23. Labeling: Key Changes 
This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for 
major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 165). The PI 
was reviewed to ensure that it meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if 
appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate 
information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise 
information for the healthcare practitioner. 
Reference ID: 5560526

===== PAGE 305 =====
NDA 219019 
QFITLIA (fitusiran) 
284 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Table 165. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations 
Full PI Sections1 
Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to 
Applicant’s Draft PI 
BOXED WARNING 
Added Boxed Warning to describe the risks of Thrombotic 
Events and Acute and Recurrent Gallbladder Disease. These 
risks are considered crucial for prescribing decisions. Patients 
developed thrombosis who did not follow bleeding management 
guidelines on the prescribed dose, so it is important for providers 
to be aware of this prior to prescribing. In addition, one patient 
on the 80 mg dose died due to thrombosis. 
For acute gallbladder disease, a significant proportion of patients 
developed cholelithiasis/cholecystitis resulting in surgery, and 
one patient had a complication of pancreatitis. It is important for 
providers to be aware of this risk prior to prescribing the drug to 
ensure prompt diagnosis/intervention. In addition, patients with a 
history of symptomatic gallbladder disease may want to consider 
alternative therapies. 
1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE 
Revised “adolescent” to “pediatric” patients and specified the 
ages of 12 years and over for clarity and per the Pediatric Use 
guidance. 
2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION 
2.1 Added AT activity monitoring recommendations as 
determined by the FDA-cleared text. Trough AT levels should be 
monitored at Weeks 4, 12, and 20, which coincide with trough 
AT levels. 
2.2 Revised Table 1 by adding a column for “dosage 
modification” to reduce the risk of dosing errors. Added Missed 
Dose instructions.  
2.3 Added new subsection to describe Bleed Management. 
Added “use during surgical interventions.” 
4 CONTRAINDICATIONS 
n/a 
5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS 
5.1 Thrombotic Events-Revised to include situations that 
increase the risk of these events and to clarify mitigating 
strategies.  
5.2. Added a new W&P for Acute and Recurrent Gallbladder 
Disease (to 5.2 as it’s in the Boxed Warning) to describe this 
serious risk, monitoring recommendations, and mitigation 
strategies. 
 5.3 Renamed 
 to “hepatotoxicity” to 
more accurately describe the risk. Revised the risk in 
consultation with the DILI team. Mitigating strategies added. 
Revised 
 and replaced 
with “avoid use in patients with moderate to severe hepatic 
impairment.” 
Deleted 
 as none 
were reported and no basis was provided.

NDA 219019 (IND 125632) 
Reviewer: R. Gudi
3
Nonclinical Summary 
Fitusiran (AD-57213 or ALN-AT3SC) is a chemically synthesized double strand small 
interfering RNA (siRNA) covalently bound to a triantennary Nacetylgalactosamine ligand 
(GalNAc) that specifically targets AT messenger RNA to lower production of AT in the 
liver. Targeting AT mRNA, fitusiran enhances thrombin generation potential and 
rebalance hemostasis in patients with hemophilia. 
The Sponsor submitted final reports for carcinogenicity studies of fitusiran 
1.  A 26-week study in TgRasH2 mouse study-at3-glp17-001.pdf, 
2.  A 2-year study in rats study-car0186.pdf.  
The mouse and rat tumor data were analyzed by FDA statistician Dr. Hepei Chen 
Statistical Review IND 125632 NDA 219019. 
In both studies, fitusiran was administered by subcutaneous injection once weekly.  In 
the mouse study, TGRasH2 mice (n=25/sex/group) were administered fitusiran doses of 
0 (0,9 % sodium chloride), 0.05, 0.15, or 0.5 mg/kg/week in males and 0 (0,9 % sodium 
chloride), 0.025, 0.08, 0.25 mg/kg/week in females. In addition, a positive control group 
of male and female TgRasH2 mice received a single, intraperitoneal dose of 75 mg/kg 
N-methyl-N-nitrosourea (MNU) on Day 1. In the rat study, Sprague-Dawley rats were 
administered fitusiran doses of 0 (0.9% Sodium Chloride), 0.03, 0.1 or 0.3 mg/kg/week 
in males, and 0 (0.9% Sodium Chloride), 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/week in females.  
The percent survival in fitusiran treated male and female mice was >96 and 100%, 
respectively; survival for the mouse vehicle control groups was 96%. Fitusiran was not 
carcinogenic in mice based on dose response-wise or pairwise comparison analysis 
conducted by both the Sponsor and the FDA statistical analysis. The positive control 
group showed statistically significant increased mortality and increases in malignant 
tumors compared to the vehicle control group. On Day 183, the Cmax values at 0.5 
mg/kg/week and 0.25 mg/kg/week were 24.3 ng/mL and 16.8 ng/mL, respectively, and 
AUC0-t values were 70.9 ng·h/mL and 28.7 ng·h/mL, respectively. The mouse to human 
exposure multiples is <1-fold the MRHD based on AUC. 
The percent survival in fitusiran treated male and female rats was >25% and >35%, 
respectively; survival for the vehicle control male and female rats was 33%, 
respectively. Fitusiran was not carcinogenic in rats based on dose response-wise or 
pairwise comparison analysis conducted by both the Sponsor and the FDA statistical 
analysis. On Day 176, in males, the Cmax and AUC0-t values at 0.3 mg/kg/week were 
91.1 ng/mL and 163 ng/mL, respectively. In females, the Cmax and AUC0-τ values at 1 
mg/kg/week were 190 ng/mL and 372 ng.h/mL, respectively. The rat to human exposure 
multiples is 1-2-fold the MRHD based on AUC.  
Reference ID: 5507497

8 Carcinogenicity
8.1 Study Title+: 
Study no.:

4.2.3.4.1

Conducting laboratory and location:

ALN-57213,  Lot # P07916, 98.5% (100 
mg/mL)
Prior Exec CAC Dose Concurrence:
Y
Basis for Dose Selection:
HD based on body weight decrements in 
the 28-day dose range findings study; LD 
and MD based on acceptable intervals.
Reviewer Carcinogenicity Conclusion (negative/positive): Negative
ECAC Carcinogenicity Conclusion: Negative
Tumor Findings:
•
There were no fitusiran-related statistically significant increase in the incidence of 
any neoplasm in RasH2 mice. 
•
There was no statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise 
comparisons in mortality in male or female RasH2 mice. 
•
The mouse to human exposure multiples is <1-fold (adjusted to bimonthly 
dosing) at the MRHD based on AUC.
•
Positive control, N-Methyl-N-Nitrosourea (MNU) significantly increased 
incidences of malignant squamous cell carcinoma and papilloma of the stomach 
in male and female RasH2 mice relative to control.  There was a statistically 
significant higher mortality rate (15/25 males, 17/25 females) for positive control 
related to tumor findings.

Gross Pathology
There were no fitusiran-related macroscopic findings observed. A low incidence of 
raised focus, nodules and/or mass observed in the stomach of control or fitusiran 
administered mice at all dose levels that generally correlated with squamous or 
glandular hyperplasia. All findings were generally consistent with the usual findings in 
the strain of RasH2 Tg/WT mice of this age.

Toxicokinetics
AUC values were not determined for LD and MD males and LD and HD females on Day 
1 and LD males and LD and HD in females on Day 183 because of insufficient 
measurable blood quantities. As a result, TK interpretation is only being performed on 
Day 183 results. Exposure on Day 183, as assessed by fitusiran Cmax values, the 
increases were generally greater than dose proportional from MD to HD, and in 
females, they were generally less than dose proportional from LD to MD and greater 
than dose proportional from MD to HD.

the study outcome.
Observations and Results
Mortality
There were several unscheduled deaths in males and females at all doses including 
control groups. There was no statistically significant dose response relationship in 
mortality compared to control groups. Two deaths, one male at MD (0.1 mg/kg/week) 
and one female at HD (1 mg/kg/week) were related to fitusiran based on necrosis in the 
brain, associated with the presence of thrombi. Although the frequency is within or 
slightly higher than the laboratory historical control data, based on the mechanism of 
action, the deaths are considered related to fitusiran. FDA statistical analysis of survival 
rate concurred that there was no statistically significant dose-response relationship or 
pairwise comparisons in mortality for both male and female rats.

Neoplastic
Neoplastic findings were noted in various tissues including control and fitusiran treated 
animals. Findings observed were considered incidental, of the nature commonly 
observed in this strain and age of rats, and/or were of similar incidence in terminal 
control and treated animals and, therefore, these findings were considered unrelated to 
the administration of fitusiran. The Applicant concluded fitusiran was not carcinogenic in

IND 125632 / NDA 219019 (ALN-AT3SC)  
 
 
 
 
 
           Page 3 
1. Background  
 
In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats 
and one in mice. These studies were to determine the carcinogenic potential of ALN-AT3SC, 
when given by subcutaneous injection once weekly for 104 consecutive weeks (total of a 104 
doses) to rats, and for 26 weeks (total of 27 doses) to the TgRasH2 hemizygous mouse. 
 
In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component (trend) of the 
effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor 
incidence rate as dose increases. 
  
2. Rat Study 
 
Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. As indicated 
in Table 3, in the experiment there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two 
hundred and fourty rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 60 rats per group. ALN-
AT3SC was administered weekly by subcutaneous injection at doses of 0.03, 0.1 or 0.3 
mg/kg/week to male rats and 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/week to female rats for a total of 104 doses. In 
this review the three treated groups were referred to as the low, mid, and high dose groups 
(Group 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and Group 5, 6, and 7 for female rats), respectively. The rats in the 
vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated with 0.9% Sodium Chloride for injection, 
USP, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups.

Due to the number of survivors, female rats of all groups (Group 1, 5, 6, and 7) and male rats of 
the low dose group (Group 2) were terminated on week 93/94; male rats of the vehicle control 
group, and the mid and high dose groups (Group 1, 3 and 4) were terminated on week 98/99. 
 
Evaluations for potential ALN-AT3SC-related effects were based on survival, clinical signs 
(including presence of palpable masses), body weights, food consumption, hematology 
parameters, toxicokinetic parameters, macroscopic observations at necropsy, and microscopic 
examinations. A complete gross pathological examination was performed on all animals, as 
specified in the Study Plan. 
 
2.1. 
Sponsor's analyses 
 
2.1.1. 
Survival analysis 
 
Reference ID: 5424371

===== PAGE 347 =====
IND 125632 / NDA 219019 (ALN-AT3SC)  
 
 
 
 
 
           Page 4 
In the sponsor’s report, the statistical analyses for mortality Main Study data were performed 
separately for each sex. The survival function of each group was estimated using the Kaplan-
Meier product-limit method applied on daily intervals and graphs were produced. Any animal 
with accidental injury that caused its death or its unscheduled sacrifice, or still alive at the day 
following the last day of the experimental period were censored in the estimation. The log-rank 
test was applied to the four groups in order to assess the significance of the overall group effect 
on mortality data. If this test was found to be significant, then, using dose level scores, the 
significance of an overall dose-related trend in mortality and pairwise comparisons between the 
vehicle control group (Group 1) and each of the test item-treated groups (Groups 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 
7) were evaluated via a Peto’s two-sided test (Peto et al., 1980). All statistical tests were 
conducted at the 5% significance level.. 
 
Sponsor’s findings:  
 
The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 
20 (33%), 15 (25%), 15 (25%), and 18 (30%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high 
dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 20 (33%), 27 (45%), 21 (35%), and 26 (43%) for 
female rats, respectively. There was no ALN-AT3SC-related increase in the incidence of 
mortalities and no statistically significant changes in mortality. 
 
2.1.2. 
Tumor data analysis 
 
In the sponsor’s analysis, the statistical evaluation of tumor data was done separately for each 
sex and was limited to subcutis and hemolymphoreticular tissue using all study animals, to all 
non-secondary neoplastic lesions found in Study Plan-required tissues/sites, and to the 
combination of hemangiosarcoma findings across whole body. Statistical analyses of 
histopathological data were conducted according to the requirements specified in the study 
plan/study plan amendment. Palpable neoplastic lesions found under skin, subcutis, and under 
Study Plan-required glands were statistically analyzed in a “mortality independent” context 
according to Peto’s onset rate using all study animals. Whereas, non-palpable neoplastic, 
incidental and fatal findings were statistically analyzed in a “mortality dependent” context 
according to Peto’s prevalence and death rate methods, respectively. For each dataset of interest, 
Peto’s test, using integer dose level scores, was conducted in order to assess significance of 
tumor rate increase across all groups and in each of Groups 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 when compared to 
Group 1. 
 
These multicentric neoplasms were shown as primary tumor only once per animal under the 
tissue “hemolymphoreticular tissue” in order to present true incidence of the given multicentric 
neoplasm for each affected animal. The presence of multiple benign tumors of the same type 
(e.g. several hepatocellular adenomas of the liver located in different lobes) was recorded only 
once, and the individual locations correlated with gross observations in the individual animal 
data record. 
 
As per Lin (Lin, 1997), the discrete permutation distribution was used for each statistical test 
involving a dataset with 10 or less tumor occurrences. All tests were performed at the 5% 
significance level and, as recommended by the FDA (Guidance for Industry, 2001), are discussed 
Reference ID: 5424371

           Page 5 
in the Anatomic Pathology Report according to the tumor prevalence classification (common or 
rare). 
 
Adjustment for multiple testing:  
 
In the sponsor’s report, no specific adjustment for multiple testing was discussed. A tumor type 
was considered rare if the spontaneous rate was 1% or less based on concurrent control data and 
historical control data from this facility or reported in the literature, otherwise a tumor type was 
classified as a common tumor. 
 
Sponsor’s findings:  
 
In the sponsor’s report, There were no ALN-AT3SC-related neoplastic microscopic findings and 
no statistical significance for palpable and non-palpable neoplasms. 
 
2.2. 
Reviewer's analyses  
 
To verify the sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing 
toxicologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses using 
the data provided by the sponsor electronically. 
 
2.2.1. 
Survival analysis 
 
In the reviewer’s analysis, the survival distributions of rats in all four groups (Groups 1, 2, 3, 4 for 
male rats, and 1, 5, 6, 7 for female rats) were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit 
method. The dose response relationship was tested across Groups 1, 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and 1, 5, 6, 
7 for female rats using the likelihood ratio test, and the homogeneity of survival distributions was 
tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A 
and 1B in the appendix for all four groups in male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent 
mortality data of all four groups and the results of the tests for dose response relationship and 
homogeneity of survivals for Groups 1, 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and 1, 5, 6, 7 for female rats are given 
in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.  
 
Reviewer’s findings:  
 
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy 
were 20 (33%), 15 (25%), 15 (25%), and 18 (30%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and 
high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 20 (33%), 27 (45%), 21 (35%), and 26 (43%) 
for female rats respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise 
comparisons in mortality was noted for both male and female rats. 
 
2.2.2. 
Tumor data analysis 
 
The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships across the vehicle control group, and 
low, mid, and high dose groups, and pairwise comparisons of each of the three treated groups 
against the vehicle control group, using the Poly-k method described in the paper of Bailer and 
Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993).  
Reference ID: 5424371

===== PAGE 349 =====
IND 125632 / NDA 219019 (ALN-AT3SC)  
 
 
 
 
 
           Page 6 
 
In the ploy-k method, the adjustment for differences in mortality among treatment groups is 
made by modifying the number of animals at risk in the denominators in the calculations of 
overall tumor rates in the Cochran-Armitage test to reflect less-than-whole-animal contributions 
for animals that die without tumor before the end of the study (Bailer and Portier 1988). The 
modification is made by defining a new number of animals at risk for each treatment group. The 
number of animals at risk for the i-th treatment group R
* i is defined as R
* i = ∑ W ij where w ij 
is the weight for the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and the sum is over all animals in the 
group. 
 
Bailer and Portier (1988) proposed the weight w ij as follows: 
wij = 1 to animals dying with the tumor, and 
wij = ( tij / tsacr )3 to animals dying without the tumor,  
where tij is the time of death of the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and tsacr is the 
planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. The above formulas imply that animals living up 
to the end of the planned terminal sacrifice date without developing any tumor will also be 
assigned wij =1 since tij = tsacr. Also animals developed the tumor type being tested before the 
end of the study will be assigned as wij = 1. 
 
Certain treatment groups of a study or the entire study may be terminated earlier than the planned 
(or intended) time of terminal sacrifice due to excessive mortalities. However, based on the 
principle of the Intention-to-treat (ITT) analysis in randomized trials, the tsacr should not be 
affected by the unplanned early terminations. The tsacr should always be equal to the planned (or 
intended) time of terminal sacrifice. For those animals that were sacrificed later than tsacr, 
regardless their actual terminal sacrifice time, tsacr was used as their time of terminal sacrifice in 
the analysis.  
 
One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the 
tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse 
studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis 
of this data.  
 
Multiple testing adjustment:  
 
For a submission with one two-year study in one species and one short-term study with another 
species, in order to keep the overall false-positive rate at the nominal level of approximately 10%, 
as indicated in Table 2, this reviewer used the methodologies suggested in the Expanded 
Statistical Decision Rules for Interpretations of Results of Rodent Carcinogenicity Studies of 
Pharmaceuticals by Lin and Rahman (2018). 
 
A rare tumor is defined as one in which the published spontaneous tumor rate is less than 1%. 
However, if the background information for the common or rare tumor is not available, the number 
of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group in the present study was used to determine 
the common or rare tumor status in the review report. That is, if the number of animals bearing 
tumors in the vehicle control group is 0, then this tumor is considered as the rare tumor; otherwise, 
if the number of animals bearing tumors in the control group is greater than or equal to 1, then this 
Reference ID: 5424371

===== PAGE 350 =====
IND 125632 / NDA 219019 (ALN-AT3SC)  
 
 
 
 
 
           Page 7 
tumor is considered as the common tumor. 
 
Table 2. Recommended decision rules for controlling the overall false positive rates 
 
 
 
Reviewer’s findings:  
 
The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 2A and 2B in the 
appendix for male and female rats, respectively. No statistically significant dose response 
relationship and pairwise comparisons were noted in tumor data for male and female rats. 
 
3. Mouse Study  
 
Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. As 
indicated in Table 3, in the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control 
group, and one positive control group. One hundred and twenty-five TgRasH2 (CByB6F1-
Tg(HRAS)2Jic) mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 25 mice per group. ALN-
AT3SC was administered weekly by subcutaneous injection at doses of 0.05, 0.15, or 0.5 
mg/kg/week to male rats,  and 025, 0.08, 0.25 mg/kg/week to female rats for a total of 27 doses. 
In this review the three treated groups were referred to as the low, mid, and high dose groups 
(Group 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and Group 5, 6, and 7 for female rats), respectively. The mice in the 
vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated once weekly by subcutaneous injection at 
0.9% sodium chloride for injection, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as 
the treated groups. The positive control group (Group 8) received a single intraperitoneal dose of 
MNU (N-Methyl-N-Nitrosurea) on Day 1 at 75 mg/kg.

The same clinical examinations, laboratory investigations and pathology procedures used in the 
rat study were performed in the mouse study. 
 
3.1. 
Sponsor's analyses 
 
Because the mouse study was conducted by the same testing facility as the rat study, the sponsor 
used the same methodologies that were used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data. 
 
3.1.1. 
Survival analysis 
 
Sponsor’s findings:  
 
The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy 
were 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 10 (40%) in the vehicle control group, the 
low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 24 
(96%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), and 8 (32%) for female mice respectively. For both 
male and female mice, there were no statistically significant differences in the incidence of 
mortality amongst the groups. 
 
3.1.2. 
Tumor data analysis 
 
Multiple testing adjustment:  
 
The same multiple testing adjustment used in the rat study was used in the mouse study.  
 
Sponsor’s findings:  
 
In the sponsor’s report, for both male and female mice, the pairwise compairons between the 
positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many 
tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as 
expected. No other statistically significant findings were noted in tumor data for male and female 
mice.

4. Summary  
 
In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats 
and one in mice. These studies were to determine the carcinogenic potential of ALN-AT3SC, 
when given by subcutaneous injection once weekly for 104 consecutive weeks (total of a 104 
doses) to rats, and for 26 weeks (total of 27 doses) to the TgRasH2 hemizygous mouse. 
 
Rat Study:  
 
Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. In each of the 
experiments, there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and 
fourty rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 60 rats per group. ALN-AT3SC was 
administered weekly by subcutaneous injection at doses of 0 (vehicle control), 0.03, 0.1 or 0.3 
mg/kg/week to male rats and 0 (vehicle control), 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/week to female rats, 
respectively. The rats in the vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated with 0.9% 
Reference ID: 5424371

===== PAGE 353 =====
IND 125632 / NDA 219019 (ALN-AT3SC)  
 
 
 
 
 
           Page 10 
Sodium Chloride for injection, USP, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner 
as the treated groups. 
 
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy 
were 20 (33%), 15 (25%), 15 (25%), and 18 (30%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and 
high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 20 (33%), 27 (45%), 21 (35%), and 26 (43%) 
for female rats respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise 
comparisons in mortality was noted for both male and female rats. 
 
In the reviewer’s analysis, no statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise 
comparisons were noted in tumor data for male and female rats. 
 
Mouse Study:  
 
Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. In each of  
the experiments there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive 
control group. One hundred and twenty-five TgRasH2 (CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic) mice of each 
sex were assigned randomly in size of 25 mice per group. ALN-AT3SC was administered 
weekly by subcutaneous injection at doses of 0.05, 0.15, or 0.5 mg/kg/week to male rats,  and 
025, 0.08, 0.25 mg/kg/week to female rats for a total of 27 doses. The mice in the vehicle control 
group (Group 1) were administrated once weekly by subcutaneous injection at 0.9% sodium 
chloride for injection, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated 
groups. The positive control group (Group 8) received a single intraperitoneal dose of MNU (N-
Methyl-N-Nitrosurea) on Day 1 at 75 mg/kg.  
 
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy 
were 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 10 (40%) in the vehicle control group, the 
low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 24 
(96%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), and 8 (32%) for female mice respectively. No 
statistically significant finding was noted in mortality for both male and female mice.  
 
In the reviewer’s analysis, for both male and female mice, the pairwise compairons between the 
positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many 
tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as 
expected. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in 
tumor data was noted in the reviewer’s analysis for both male and female mice.

mortality on tests for carcinogenicity in small samples.” Biometrics, 44, 417-431.

tests for increased carcinogenicity”. Biometrics 49, 793-801.

Rodent Carcinogenicity Statues of Pharmaceuticals (Draft Guidance). U.S. Department of Health 
and Human Services, Food and Drug Administration, Center for Drug Evaluation and Research 
(CDER). May 2001.

pp.19-57.

Biopharmaceutical Statistics, ed. Shein-Chung Chow, Marcel Dekker, New York. 
Lin K.K. and Rahman A.M. (1998). ” Overall false positive rates in tests for linear trend in tumor 
incidence in animal carcinogenicity studies of new drugs”, Journal of Biopharmaceutical 
Statistics, 8(1), 1-15. 
Lin, K.K., M.A. Rahman (2018), "Chapter 8: Expanded Statistical Decision Rules for 
Interpretations of Results of Rodent Carcinogenicity Studies of Pharmaceuticals", IN 
Biopham1aceutical Applied Statistics Symposiu m, Volume 3: Phannaceutical Applications, 
Editors: Peace, Karl E., Chen, Ding-Geng, Menon, Sandeep (Eds.), recently published by 
Springer in Au gust 2018, Pages 151-183.

(1980) “Guidelines for sample sensitive significance test for carcinogenic effects in long-term
